\tau\in\Gamma_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
, then

|\eta(t)|<\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3},\ \ |w(t)|<\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3},|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(t)|<\mu_{1}\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-9/2},

(11.27)

if
\tau\in(D_{q,r}\setminus(D_{q,r,d}\cup\bar{D}_{q,r,d})\cap\{\operatorname{Im}\tau\geq-c_{l,1}^{-1}\}
, then

\displaystyle

\displaystyle|\beta_{1}|<0.5\varepsilon^{3}c_{r,1}{d}_{+}^{-7/2},\ |\beta_{2}|<0.5\varepsilon^{3}c_{r,2}{d}_{+}^{-3},

(11.28)

and

\displaystyle

\displaystyle|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(t)|<\mu_{1}\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-9/2},

(11.29)

\displaystyle 0.5\hat{d}_{+}(\varepsilon t)\leq d_{+}(t)\leq 2\hat{d}_{+}(\varepsilon t).

if
\tau\in(D_{q,r}\setminus(D_{q,r,d}\cup\bar{D}_{q,r,d})\cap\{\operatorname{Im}\tau\leq c_{l,1}^{-1}\}
, then

\displaystyle|\beta_{4}|<0.5\varepsilon^{3}c_{r,4}{d}_{-}^{-3},

(11.30)

Here
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}
are positive constants whose values do not depend on the choice of the constant
C_{q}
, provided that
C_{q}
is sufficiently large. The values
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}
are determined after the statement of Lemma
11.6
.

Note that inequalities (
11.23
) - (
11.30
) are certainly satisfied for sufficiently small
T-t_{c}
. Also note that
\kappa(t)=\overline{\kappa(\bar{t})},\eta(t)=\overline{\eta(\bar{t})},\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(t)=\overline{\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\bar{t})}
.

Assumption (
11.29
) implies that

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{1}(\kappa(t))-\Lambda_{1}(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t))||z|=\mu_{1}\varepsilon^{4}O(\hat{d}_{\pm}^{-5})\varepsilon^{1/3}<0.1c_{r,1}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{\pm}^{-7/2},

\displaystyle|B(\kappa(t))-B(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t))||\eta|=\mu_{1}\varepsilon^{4}O(\hat{d}_{\pm}^{-5})\varepsilon^{1/3}<0.1c_{r,2}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{\pm}^{-3}

\displaystyle\mbox{ (``+'' for $\operatorname{Im}\tau\geq-c_{l,1}^{-1}$,
``-'' for $\operatorname{Im}\tau\leq c_{l,1}^{-1}$).}

(Here we used the estimate
|\xi|<c_{t,3}\varepsilon^{1/3}
.)

Thus, equations (
11.21
) have the form

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\dot{z}=\Lambda_{1}(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon})z+\varepsilon^{3}\tilde{O}_{1}(d_{+}^{-7/2}),

(11.31)

\displaystyle\dot{\eta}=B(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon})\eta+\varepsilon^{3}\tilde{O}_{2}(d_{+}^{-3})

with
|\tilde{O}_{1}(d_{+}^{-7/2})|<2c_{r,1}d_{+}^{-7/2},\ |\tilde{O}_{2}(d_{+}^{-3})|<2c_{r,2}d_{+}^{-3}
.

Equation (
11.22
) has the form

\displaystyle\dot{z}=\Lambda_{1}(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon})z+\varepsilon^{3}\tilde{O}_{4}(d_{-}^{-3})

(11.32)

with
|\tilde{O}_{4}(d_{-}^{3})|<2c_{r,4}d_{-}^{-3}
.

Lemma 11.3

Consider in the domain
D_{q,r}
a segment of vertical line drawn downward from a point on
\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon}
. Let
\hat{d}_{u}
denote the value of
\hat{d}_{+}
at the upper endpoint of this segment. For sufficiently large
C_{q}
, if the length of this segment is less than or equal to
c_{r,5}^{-1}\hat{d}_{u}
, then on this segment

0.5\hat{d}_{u}<\hat{d}_{+}<2\hat{d}_{u}.

Lemma 11.4

For sufficiently large
C_{q}
, the vertical width of the domain
D_{q,r,d}
satisfies the conditions of Lemma
11.3
, namely

c_{e,12}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-1/2}|\ln(c_{e,11}^{-1}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})|<c_{r,5}^{-1}\hat{d}_{u}.

Lemma 11.5

By choosing
C_{q}
sufficiently large, the tangent directions of the curve
\tilde{\Gamma}_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
and of the curve
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
passing through the same point can be made arbitrary close to each other. The same holds for the curve
\Gamma_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
.

Lemma 11.6

For
\tau\in S(T)
, we have the following estimates.

If
\tau\in D_{q,r,d}
, then

|z(t)|<c_{r,6}\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2},\ |\eta(t)|<c_{r,7}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3},\ |w(t)|<c_{r,7}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3},|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|<c_{r,8}\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-9/2};

additionally, if
\tau\in D_{q,r,d}\setminus\tilde{D}_{q,r,d}
,
then

|z(t)|<c_{r,9}(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4}),\ |\eta(t)|<c_{r,10}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3},\ |w(t)|<c_{r,10}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}
,

additionally, if
\tau\in D_{q,r,d}^{\prime}\setminus\tilde{D}_{q,r,d}
,
then

|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|<c_{r,11}\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-9/2}
,

additionally, if
\tau\in\Gamma_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
, then

|z(t)|<c_{r,12}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4}
.

If
\tau\in\bar{D}_{q,r,d}
, then

|z(t)|<c_{r,7}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{-,q}^{-3},\ |\eta(t)|<c_{r,7}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{-,q}^{-3},\ |w(t)|<c_{r,6}\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{-,q}^{-5/2},\\
|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|<c_{r,8}\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{-,q}^{-9/2}
,

additionally, if
\tau\in\bar{D}_{q,r,d}\setminus\overline{\tilde{D}}_{q,r,d}
, then

|z(t)|<c_{r,13}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{-}^{-3},\ |\eta(t)|<c_{r,13}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{-}^{-3},\\
|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|<c_{r,14}\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{-}^{-9/2}
.

If
\tau\in(D_{q,r}\setminus(D_{q,r,d}\cup\bar{D}_{q,r,d}))\cap\{\operatorname{Im}\tau>-c_{l,1}^{-1}\}
, then

|z(t)|<c_{r,15}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4},\ |\eta(t)|<c_{r,16}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3},\ |\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|<c_{r,17}\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-9/2}.

If
\tau\in(D_{q,r}\setminus(D_{q,r,d}\cup\bar{D}_{q,r,d}))\cap\{\operatorname{Im}\tau>-c_{l,1}^{-1}\}
, then

|z(t)|<c_{r,16}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{-}^{-3},\ |\eta(t)|<c_{r,16}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{-}^{-3},\ |\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|<c_{r,17}\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{-}^{-9/2}.

The constants
c_{r,6},c_{r,7},\ldots,c_{r,17}
do not depend on the value
T
or on the choice of the constants
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}
.

We take
\mu_{1}=2\max\{c_{r,8},c_{r,11},c_{r,14},c_{r,17}\},\mu_{2}=2c_{r,9}
.

Lemma 11.7

If the constant
c_{e,10}
is chosen sufficiently large, then for any
\varepsilon T<\tau_{q,\varepsilon,+}
the assumptions (
11.23
) - (
11.30
) are satisfied with a margin.

Thus, one can take
\varepsilon T=\tau_{q,\varepsilon,+}
. Then, the estimates in Lemma
11.6
imply the estimates in Lemma
6.8
. Note that
\varepsilon^{2}d_{+,q}^{-5/2}=O(\varepsilon^{1/3}C_{q}^{-5/2}),\ \varepsilon^{3}d_{+,q}^{-3}=O(\varepsilon C_{q}^{-3}),\ \varepsilon^{4}d_{+,q}^{-9/2}=O(\varepsilon C_{q}^{-9/2})
.

This completes the proof of Lemma
6.8
.

\square

Proof of Lemma
6.9
.

According to (
5.7
), in the domain
D_{q,r,d}
we have

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\dot{\kappa}=\varepsilon F(\kappa)+\varepsilon^{2}O(|z|^{2}d_{+}^{-2})+\varepsilon^{2}O(|\xi|_{*}^{2}d_{+}^{-3/2})

(11.33)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon O\left(|z|^{4}+|z|^{5}d_{+}^{-2}+|\xi|_{*}^{3}\right)+\varepsilon^{3}O(|z|d_{+}^{-3}+|\xi|_{*}d_{+}^{-3/2})+\varepsilon^{4}O(d_{+}^{-7/2}|\xi|).

On
\Gamma_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
, we have

|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|<c_{r,15}\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-9/2}.

(11.34)

We estimate
|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|
in
D_{q,r,d}
by moving vertically upward from
\Gamma_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
. The vertical width of
D_{q,r,d}
is
c_{e,12}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-1/2}|\ln(c_{e,11}^{-1}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})|
. We know that

\partial F(\kappa)/\partial\kappa=O(\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2})
.
According to Lemmas
11.3
and
11.4
, on the vertical line,
d_{u}
can be replaced by
\hat{d}_{+}
in
O(\cdot)
-estimates.
Then, for
\tau\in D_{q,r,d}
, equations (
11.33
) and (
11.34
) imply

\displaystyle|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|=O(\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-9/2})

\displaystyle+(c_{e,12}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}|\ln(c_{e,11}^{-1}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})|)O\left(\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{1/3}C_{q}^{-5/2})^{2}d_{+}^{-2})+\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{1/3}C_{q}^{-5/2})(\varepsilon C_{q}^{-3})d_{+}^{-3/2}\right.

\displaystyle+((\varepsilon^{1/3}C_{q}^{-5/2})^{4}+(\varepsilon^{1/3}C_{q}^{-5/2})^{5}d_{+}^{-2}+(\varepsilon^{1/3}C_{q}^{-5/2})^{2}(\varepsilon C_{q}^{-3})+\varepsilon^{2}(\varepsilon^{1/3}C_{q}^{-5/2})d_{+}^{-3}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}(\varepsilon C_{q}^{-3})d_{+}^{-3/2})\left.+\varepsilon^{3}(\varepsilon^{1/3}C_{q}^{-5/2})d_{+}^{-7/2}\right)

\displaystyle=O(\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-9/2})+(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}|\ln(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})|)

\displaystyle\cdot O\left(\varepsilon^{5/3}C_{q}^{-5}d_{+}^{-2}+\varepsilon^{7/3}C_{q}^{-11/2}d_{+}^{-3/2}+\varepsilon^{4/3}C_{q}^{-10}+\varepsilon^{5/3}C_{q}^{-25/2}d_{+}^{-2}+\varepsilon^{5/3}C_{q}^{-8}+\varepsilon^{7/3}C_{q}^{-5/2}d_{+}^{-3}\right.

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{3}C_{q}^{-3}d_{+}^{-3/2}\left.+\varepsilon^{10/3}C_{q}^{-5/2}d_{+}^{-7/2}\right)

\displaystyle=O(\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-9/2})+(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}|\ln(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})|)

\displaystyle\cdot O\left(\varepsilon^{5/3}C_{q}^{-5}d_{+}^{-2}+\varepsilon^{4/3}C_{q}^{-10}+\varepsilon^{7/3}C_{q}^{-5/2}d_{+}^{-3}\right)

Thus,

\displaystyle|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|<c_{e,26}\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-9/2})+c_{e,27}(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}|\ln(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})|)

\displaystyle\cdot\left(\varepsilon^{5/3}C_{q}^{-5}d_{+}^{-2}+\varepsilon^{4/3}C_{q}^{-10}+\varepsilon^{7/3}C_{q}^{-5/2}d_{+}^{-3}\right).

\square

Proof of Lemma
6.10
.

For a point
\tau_{u}\in\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon}
, we denote
\hat{d}_{u}=\hat{d}(\tau_{u}),\Lambda_{1,u}=\Lambda_{1}(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{u})),\rho=-c_{e,12}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-1/2}\ln(c_{e,11}^{-1}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-3/2})
. Denote
\tau_{v}=\tau_{u}-i\rho\in\Gamma_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
.
Fix some
\tau_{u}=\tau_{u,*}\in\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon}
and the corresponding values

\hat{d}_{u}=\hat{d}_{u,*},\Lambda_{1,u}=\Lambda_{1,*},\rho=\rho_{*}
. Consider the level curve
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\tau)={\rm const}
passing through
the point
\tau_{v,*}=\tau_{u,*}-i\rho_{*}
. Denote by
\tau_{\psi}
the point on this curve lying below point
\tau_{u}
(i.e.
\operatorname{Im}\tau_{\psi}=\operatorname{Im}\tau_{u}
). Denote
i\delta=\tau_{u}-\tau_{\psi}
.

We have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{\psi})=\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{v,*})=\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{u,*}-i\rho_{*})=\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{u,*})-\operatorname{Re}(i\Lambda_{1,*})\rho_{*}+O(\rho_{*}^{2}d_{u,*}^{-1/2})

(11.35)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{u,*})+\operatorname{Im}(\Lambda_{1,*})\rho_{*}+O(\rho_{*}^{2}d_{u,*}^{-1/2}).

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{\psi})=\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{u}-i\delta)=\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{u})-\operatorname{Re}(i\Lambda_{1})\delta+O(\delta^{2}d_{u}^{-1/2})

(11.36)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{u})+\operatorname{Im}(\Lambda_{1})\delta+O(\delta^{2}d_{u}^{-1/2})=\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{u,*})+\operatorname{Im}(\Lambda_{1})\delta+O(\delta^{2}d_{u}^{-1/2}).

Thus,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{u,*})+\operatorname{Im}(\Lambda_{1,*})\rho_{*}+O(\rho_{*}^{2}d_{u,*}^{-1/2})=\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{u,*})+\operatorname{Im}(\Lambda_{1})\delta+O(\delta^{2}d_{u}^{-1/2}).

(11.37)

In what follows, we will proceed to the limit as
\tau_{u}
tends to
\tau_{u,*}
. Thus, we can replace
\rho_{*}
with
\delta
, and
d_{u,*}
with
d_{u}
in
O(\cdot)
-symbols. We obtain

\displaystyle\delta=\frac{\operatorname{Im}(\Lambda_{1,*})}{\operatorname{Im}(\Lambda_{1})}\rho_{*}+O(\rho_{*}^{2}d_{u}^{-1}).

(11.38)

Then

\displaystyle\delta-\rho=\frac{\operatorname{Im}(\Lambda_{1,*})}{\operatorname{Im}(\Lambda_{1})}\rho_{*}-\rho+O(\rho_{*}^{2}d_{u}^{-1})=\left(\frac{\operatorname{Im}(\Lambda_{1,*})}{\operatorname{Im}(\Lambda_{1})}-\frac{\rho}{\rho_{*}}\right)\rho_{*}+O(\rho_{*}^{2}d_{u}^{-1})

(11.39)

\displaystyle=\frac{\operatorname{Im}(\Lambda_{1,*})}{\operatorname{Im}(\Lambda_{1})}\left(1-\frac{\operatorname{Im}(\Lambda_{1})\hat{d}_{u}^{-1/2}\ln(c_{e,11}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-3/2})}{\operatorname{Im}(\Lambda_{1,*})\hat{d}_{u,*}^{-1/2}\ln(c_{e,11}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u,*}^{-3/2})}\right)\rho_{*}+O(\rho_{*}^{2}d_{u}^{-1}).

We have
\operatorname{Im}(\Lambda_{1})=\alpha(\hat{d}_{u})\hat{d}_{u}^{1/2}+O(\hat{d}_{u})
, where
\alpha
is a smooth function. Differentiate
\delta-\rho
over
\hat{d}_{u}
at
\hat{d}_{u}=\hat{d}_{u,*}
. Principal part of the derivative for small
\hat{d}_{u}
is given by differentiation of the logarithm. We have

\displaystyle\left(\frac{d(\delta-\rho)}{d(\hat{d}_{u})}\right)_{\hat{d}_{u}=\hat{d}_{u,*}}=\frac{3}{2}\frac{\rho_{*}}{\hat{d}_{u,*}\ln(c_{e,11}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u,*}^{-3/2})}+O(\hat{d}_{u,*}^{-1/2}\rho_{*})+O(\rho_{*}^{2}d_{u,*}^{-2}).

(11.40)

The first term in this derivative is dominant provided that
\hat{d}_{u,*}<c_{e,11,3}/{\ln\varepsilon)^{2}}
and
C_{q}
is sufficiently large. In this case, since value of the logarithm is negative, the value
(\delta-\rho)
decreases from 0 (which is its value at
\hat{d}_{u}=\hat{d}_{u,*}
).
This means that curves
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
cross the curve
\Gamma_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
in the upward direction as
\operatorname{Re}\tau
increases.

\square

12
Proofs of lemmas about motion.

Proof of Lemma
7.1
.

Recall that
\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{*}^{-})=\kappa(\tau_{*}^{-}/\varepsilon)
,
z(\tau_{*}^{-}/\varepsilon)=O(\varepsilon^{3}),\eta(\tau_{*}^{-}/\varepsilon)=O(\varepsilon^{3})
, and
\varepsilon t_{3}=\tau_{*,\varepsilon,-}(\tau_{\gamma})=\tau_{*}^{-}+O(\varepsilon\ln\varepsilon)
. For
t
between
\tau_{*}^{-}/\varepsilon
and
t_{3}
, we have
z(t)=O(\varepsilon^{3}),\eta(t)=O(\varepsilon^{3})
. Thus, during this time interval, according to (
5.7
),

\dot{\kappa}=\varepsilon F(\kappa)+O(\varepsilon^{6}),

while

\dot{\mathrm{K}}_{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon F(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}).

Thus, during the considered time interval we have
\kappa(t)=\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)+O(\varepsilon^{6}\ln\varepsilon
).

\square

Proof of Lemma
7.2
.

For
\operatorname{Im}\tau>-c_{l,1}^{-1}
, we have

\displaystyle C^{-1}(\mathrm{K}(\varepsilon t))(x(t)-X(\mathrm{K}(\varepsilon t)))=C^{-1}(\mathrm{K}(\varepsilon t))C(\kappa(t))C^{-1}(\kappa(t))(x(t)-X(\mathrm{K}(\varepsilon t)))

(12.1)

\displaystyle=C^{-1}(\mathrm{K}(\varepsilon t))C(\kappa(t))\left[C^{-1}(\kappa(t))\left(x(t)-X(\kappa(t))\right)+C^{-1}(\kappa(t))\left(X(\kappa(t))-X(\mathrm{K}(\varepsilon t))\right)\right]

The estimate (
7.2
) and Lemma
6.1
imply that

C^{-1}(\mathrm{K}(\varepsilon t))C(\kappa(t))=I+\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}\left(O(\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3})+O(\varepsilon(1+|\ln\hat{d}_{+})|)\right),

where
I
is the unit matrix. We use here the fact that the derivative of
C
with respect to
\kappa
is
O(\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2})
.

We have
C^{-1}(\kappa(t))\left(x(t)-X(\kappa(t))\right)=\tilde{\xi}(t)
, where
\tilde{\xi}(t)
is the vector introduced in Lemma
5.2
. For components of this vector, we have

\tilde{z}(t)=O(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-1}),\ \tilde{w}(t)=O(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}),\ \tilde{\eta}(t)=O(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}).

For components of the vector
C^{-1}(\kappa(t))(X(\kappa(t))-X(\mathrm{K}(\varepsilon t)))=\xi^{\prime}(t)
, we have

\displaystyle z^{\prime}(t)=\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}\left(O(\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3})+O(\varepsilon(1+|\ln\hat{d}_{+})|)\right)=O(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-1}),

\displaystyle|w^{\prime}(t)|+|\eta^{\prime}(t)|=\left(O(\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3})+O(\varepsilon(1+|\ln\hat{d}_{+})|)\right)=O(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}).

We use here that, according to (
2.1
), the derivative of the
z_{1}
(respectively,
z_{2}
) component of the equilibrium of the fast system with respect to
\kappa
is
O(\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2})
(respectively,
O(1)
).

Combining above estimates, we obtain the result of the lemma for
\operatorname{Im}\tau>-c_{l,1}^{-1}
.
Similar estimates give the result of the lemma for
\operatorname{Im}\tau<c_{l,1}^{-1}
.

\square

Proof of Lemma
7.3
.

According to Lemma
4.9
,

\frac{\partial\hat{\zeta}}{\partial R}=-i\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{2\pi\hat{s}}\frac{1}{(RJ_{-1/3}(v)+J_{1/3}(v))^{2}},\quad v=\frac{2}{3}(-\hat{s})^{3/2}.

(12.2)

Thus, according to asymptotic formula (
4.9
),

|\frac{\partial\hat{\zeta}}{\partial R}|_{R=e^{2\pi i/3}}>c_{a,1}\frac{v}{|\hat{s}|}\sim\frac{|\hat{s}|^{3/2}}{|\hat{s}|}>c_{a,2}|\hat{s}|^{1/2}.

Therefore,

R_{\gamma}-R_{-}=O(|z_{1,\varepsilon}(t_{\gamma})-z_{1,sp}(t_{\gamma})|/|\tau_{\gamma}-\tau_{c}|^{1/2})=O(|z_{1,\varepsilon}(t_{\gamma})-z_{1,sp}(t_{\gamma})|/(C_{\gamma,*}^{1/2}\varepsilon^{1/3})).

\square

Proof of Lemma
7.4
.

According to Lemma
4.9
and the asymptotic formula (
4.9
),

|\frac{\partial\hat{\zeta}}{\partial R}|_{R=e^{2\pi i/3}}>c_{a,1}|\hat{s}|^{1/2}.

On the other hand, formula (
4.9
) implies that

|\frac{\partial\hat{\zeta}}{\partial\hat{s}}|_{R=e^{2\pi i/3}}=O(|\hat{s}|^{-1/2}),\quad|\frac{\partial\hat{\zeta}}{\partial\tau}|_{R=e^{2\pi i/3}}=O(\varepsilon^{-2/3}|\hat{s}|^{-1/2}).

Thus

\displaystyle R_{\varepsilon}(t_{\gamma})-R_{\gamma}=O(|\tau_{\varepsilon}(t_{\gamma})-\tau_{\gamma}|/(\varepsilon^{-2/3}|\hat{s}|))=O(|\tau_{\varepsilon}(t_{\gamma})-\tau_{\gamma}|/(|\tau_{\gamma}-\tau_{c}|)

\displaystyle=O(|\tau_{\varepsilon}(t_{\gamma})-\tau_{\gamma}|/(C_{\gamma,*}\varepsilon^{2/3})).

\square

Proof of Lemma
7.5
.

Consider the equations for the variables
z_{sm},\eta_{sm}
, and
\kappa
for
t\in D_{\triangle}
. These equations have the form

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\dot{z}_{sm}=b\cdot(\kappa-\kappa_{c})+az_{sm}^{2}+O(\varepsilon+|z_{sm}|^{3}+|\kappa-\kappa_{c}|^{2}+|\kappa-\kappa_{c}||\xi_{sm}|+|\xi_{sm}|_{*}^{2}),

(12.3)

\displaystyle\dot{\eta}_{sm}=B\eta_{sm}+O(\varepsilon+|\kappa-\kappa_{c}|+|\kappa-\kappa_{c}||\xi_{sm}|+|\xi_{sm}|^{2}),

\displaystyle\dot{\kappa}=\varepsilon(g_{c}+O(\varepsilon+|\kappa-\kappa_{c}|+|\xi_{sm}|)).

Initial conditions for this system are taken at
t=t_{\gamma}
in accordance with values of
z,\eta,w,\kappa
at

t_{\gamma}
:

z_{sm}(t_{\gamma})=z(t_{\gamma}),\eta_{sm}(t_{\gamma})=\eta(t_{\gamma}),w_{sm}(t_{\gamma})=w(t_{\gamma}).

Thus, we have

z_{sm}(t_{\gamma})<c_{t,1}\varepsilon^{1/3},\eta_{sm}(t_{\gamma})<c_{t,2}\varepsilon^{2/3},w_{sm}(t_{\gamma})<c_{t,3}\varepsilon^{2/3},|\kappa(t_{\gamma})-\kappa_{c}|<c_{t,4}\varepsilon^{2/3}.

Discarding in (
12.3
)
O(\cdot)
terms, we obtain the system

\dot{\hat{z}}_{sm}=b\cdot(\hat{\kappa}-\kappa_{c})+a\hat{z}_{sm}^{2},\ \dot{\hat{\eta}}_{sm}=B\hat{\eta}_{sm},\ \dot{\hat{\kappa}}=\varepsilon g_{c}.

(12.4)

Solutions of these equations for
{\hat{z}}_{sm},\hat{\kappa}
with the same initial conditions as for (
12.3
) are well defined in
D_{\triangle}
and satisfy there the estimates

|\hat{z}_{sm}|<c_{t,5}\varepsilon^{1/3},|\hat{\kappa}-\kappa_{c}|<c_{t,7}\varepsilon^{2/3}.

(12.5)

This is evident for
\hat{\kappa}
. For
\hat{z}_{sm}
, this follows from the explicit formulas for the solution in Section
4.2
.

For
{\hat{\eta}}_{sm}
, for each
t_{u}
from the lower boundary of
D_{\triangle}
, consider the solution
{\hat{\eta}}_{sm,t_{u}}
with the initial condition
{\hat{\eta}}_{sm,t_{u}}(t_{u})=\eta(t_{u})
. Consider this solution when the time changes along the curve
\operatorname{Re}\psi_{a}(\tau)={\rm const}
passing through
t_{u}
; the function
\psi_{a}
is introduced in Section
4.2
. This solution satisfies a linear ODE system with constant coefficients whose eigenvalues have negative real parts. Therefore, the solution is well defined on the whole considered curve
\operatorname{Re}\psi_{a}(\tau)={\rm const}
in
D_{\triangle}
, and satisfies the estimate

|{\hat{\eta}}_{sm,t_{u}}|<c_{t,6}\varepsilon^{2/3}.

(12.6)

An analogous construction can be performed in the domain

\bar{D}_{\triangle}
. We denote corresponding variables

z_{ms},\eta_{ms},w_{ms},\xi_{ms},\hat{z}_{ms},\hat{\eta}_{ms,t_{u}},\hat{\xi}_{ms,t_{u}},\hat{\kappa}
. The estimates are

|\hat{w}_{ms}|<c_{t,5}\varepsilon^{1/3},|{\hat{\eta}}_{ms,t_{u}}|<c_{t,6}\varepsilon^{2/3},|\hat{\kappa}-\bar{\kappa}_{c}|<c_{t,7}\varepsilon^{2/3}.

(12.7)

Denote by
S(T)
the part of
D_{c}
where
\operatorname{Re}t\leq T
. The solution of system (
5.7
) can be continued at least into
S(T_{1})
such that in this domain, in
D_{\triangle}
, we have

|z_{sm}|<2c_{t,5}\varepsilon^{1/3},|{\eta}_{sm}|<2c_{t,6}\varepsilon^{2/3},|\kappa-\kappa_{c}|<2c_{t,7}\varepsilon^{2/3},|w_{sm}|=O(\varepsilon^{r}),

(12.8)

and in
\bar{D}_{\triangle}
we have

|w_{ms}|<2c_{t,5}\varepsilon^{1/3},|{\eta}_{ms}|<2c_{t,6}\varepsilon^{2/3},|\kappa-\bar{\kappa}_{c}|<2c_{t,7}\varepsilon^{2/3},|z_{ms}|=O(\varepsilon^{r}).

(12.9)

Here
r=1/2
.

Lemma 12.1

For
\operatorname{Re}t\leq T_{1}
, we have

\kappa(\bar{t})=\overline{\kappa(t)},\ z_{sm}(\bar{t})=\overline{w_{ms}(t)},\ w_{sm}(\bar{t})=\overline{w_{ms}(t)},\ \eta_{sm}(\bar{t})=\overline{\eta_{ms}(t)}.

(12.10)

On the boundary, where
\operatorname{Im}\tau=\operatorname{Im}\tau_{\gamma}
, we have

\displaystyle

\displaystyle z_{sm}=(1+O(\varepsilon^{1/3}))z+O(\varepsilon^{1/3})w+O(\varepsilon^{1/3})\eta+O(\varepsilon^{1/3}),

(12.11)

\displaystyle w_{sm}=(1+O(\varepsilon^{1/3}))w+O(\varepsilon^{1/3})z+O(\varepsilon^{1/3})\eta+O(\varepsilon^{2/3}).

Similarly, on the boundary, where
\operatorname{Im}\tau=-\operatorname{Im}\tau_{\gamma}
, we have

\displaystyle

\displaystyle z_{ms}=(1+O(\varepsilon^{1/3}))z+O(\varepsilon^{1/3})w+O(\varepsilon^{1/3})\eta+O(\varepsilon^{2/3}),

(12.12)

\displaystyle w_{ms}=(1+O(\varepsilon^{1/3}))w+O(\varepsilon^{1/3})z+O(\varepsilon^{1/3})\eta+O(\varepsilon^{1/3}).

Using estimates (
12.8
), (
12.9
), and considering equations (
12.3
) as a perturbation of equations (
12.4
), we can estimate
z_{sm},\eta_{sm}
and
w_{ms},\eta_{ms}
for
\varepsilon t\in D_{\triangle},\operatorname{Re}t\leq T_{1}
:

\displaystyle|z_{sm}(t)|<1.5c_{t,5}\varepsilon^{1/3},|{\eta}_{sm}(t)|<1.5c_{t,6}\varepsilon^{2/3},|\kappa(t)-\kappa_{c}|<1.5c_{t,7}\varepsilon^{2/3},

(12.13)

\displaystyle|w_{ms}(\bar{t})|<1.5c_{t,5}\varepsilon^{1/3},|{\eta}_{ms}(\bar{t})|<1.5c_{t,6}\varepsilon^{2/3},|\kappa(\bar{t})-\bar{\kappa}_{c}|<1.5c_{t,7}\varepsilon^{2/3}.

On the lower boundary of
D_{up}
, which is also the upper boundary of
\bar{D}_{\triangle}
, we have

|z(t)|=O(\varepsilon^{2/3}),|w(t)|=O(\varepsilon^{1/3}),\eta(t)=O(\varepsilon^{2/3})
and, therefore
|z_{ms}(t)|=O(\varepsilon^{2/3}),|w_{ms}(t))|=O(\varepsilon^{1/3}),\eta_{ms}(t)=O(\varepsilon^{2/3})
.
Considering motion vertically downward from this boundary, we obtain
|z_{ms}(t)|=O(\varepsilon^{2/3})
for
\varepsilon t\in\bar{D}_{\triangle},\operatorname{Re}t\leq T_{1}
. According to (
12.10
),
|w_{sm}(t)|=|z_{ms}(\bar{t})
. Thus,

|w_{sm}(t)|=O(\varepsilon^{2/3})
for
\varepsilon t\in D_{\triangle},\operatorname{Re}t\leq T_{1}
. Therefore, conditions (
12.8
), (
12.9
) are satisfied at
\operatorname{Re}t=T_{1}
with a margin. Therefore, one can take
T_{1}=\operatorname{Re}\tau_{c}
. Additionally to these estimates, we obtain
|w_{sm}(t)|=O(\varepsilon^{2/3})
for
t\in D_{\triangle}
, and
|z_{ms}(t)|=O(\varepsilon^{2/3})
for
\varepsilon t\in\bar{D}_{\triangle}
.

For
\varepsilon t\in D_{\triangle}
, we have

\dot{\kappa}=\varepsilon(g_{c}+O(\varepsilon+|\kappa-\kappa_{c}|+|\xi_{sm}|)),

which implies

\dot{\kappa}=\varepsilon(g_{c}+O(\varepsilon^{1/3})).

For
\mathrm{K}
in the considered domain we have

\dot{\mathrm{K}}=\varepsilon(g_{c}+O(\varepsilon^{1/3})).

We also have
\kappa(\tau_{\gamma}/\varepsilon)=\mathrm{K}(\tau_{\gamma})+O(\varepsilon\ln\varepsilon)
. Thus, we have

\kappa(t)=\mathrm{K}(\tau)+O(\varepsilon\ln\varepsilon)

for
\varepsilon t\in D_{\triangle}
. This also implies
\kappa(\tau_{c}/\varepsilon)=\kappa_{c}+O(\varepsilon\ln\varepsilon)
.

Denote by
\hat{s}_{(\gamma)}
the value of
\hat{s}
at
\tau=\tau_{\gamma}
. Then

\hat{s}_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{\gamma}/\varepsilon)=\hat{s}_{(\gamma)}+O(\varepsilon^{1/3}|\ln\varepsilon|+\varepsilon^{1/3}C_{\gamma,*}^{3/2})
. We also have (proven below)

\frac{d\hat{s}_{\varepsilon}}{d\hat{s}}=1+O(\varepsilon^{1/3}).

This implies that
\hat{s}_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{c}/\varepsilon)=O(\varepsilon^{1/3}|\ln\varepsilon|+\varepsilon^{1/3}C_{\gamma,*}^{3/2})
.

Differentiate the value
\hat{\zeta}_{\varepsilon}(t)
(
7.6
) with respect to time
\hat{s}
. On the one hand, using the already obtained estimates in
D_{\triangle}
, we obtain

\frac{d{\hat{\zeta}}_{\varepsilon}(t)}{d\hat{s}}=i(\varepsilon^{-2/3}\mu b\cdot(\kappa(t)-\kappa_{c})/(b\cdot g_{c})+({\hat{\zeta}}_{\varepsilon}(t))^{2})+O(\varepsilon^{1/3})=i(\hat{s}_{\varepsilon}+({\hat{\zeta}}_{\varepsilon}(t))^{2})+O(\varepsilon^{1/3}).

(12.14)

Here
\mu
is a complex number such that
\hat{s}=\varepsilon^{-2/3}\mu(\tau-\tau_{c})
.
On the other hand, using Lemma
4.9
, we obtain

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\frac{d{\hat{\zeta}}_{\varepsilon}(t)}{d\hat{s}}=\frac{\partial{\hat{\zeta}}_{\varepsilon}(t)}{\partial R_{\varepsilon}}\frac{dR_{\varepsilon}}{d\hat{s}}+\frac{\partial{\hat{\zeta}}_{\varepsilon}(t)}{\partial\hat{s}_{\varepsilon}}\frac{d\hat{s}_{\varepsilon}}{d\hat{s}}

(12.15)

\displaystyle=-i\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{2\pi\hat{s}_{\varepsilon}}\frac{1}{(R_{\varepsilon}J_{-1/3}(v_{\varepsilon}(t))+J_{1/3}(v_{\varepsilon}(t)))^{2}}\frac{dR_{\varepsilon}}{d\hat{s}}+i(\hat{s}_{\varepsilon}+({\hat{\zeta}}_{\varepsilon}(t))^{2})\frac{d\hat{s}_{\varepsilon}}{d\hat{s}}.

Also

\frac{d\hat{s}_{\varepsilon}}{d\hat{s}}=\frac{d\tau_{\varepsilon}}{d\tau}=\frac{d(g_{c}(\kappa(t)-\kappa_{c})/(b\cdot g_{c}))}{d\tau}=1+O(\varepsilon^{1/3}).

(12.16)

Comparing (
12.14
) and (
12.15
), we obtain

\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{2\pi\hat{s}_{\varepsilon}}\frac{1}{(R_{\varepsilon}J_{-1/3}(v_{\varepsilon}(t))+J_{1/3}(v_{\varepsilon}(t)))^{2}}\frac{dR_{\varepsilon}}{d\hat{s}}=O(\varepsilon^{1/3}).

(12.17)

Thus,

\frac{dR_{\varepsilon}}{d\hat{s}}=O(\varepsilon^{1/3}\hat{s}_{\varepsilon}(R_{\varepsilon}J_{-1/3}(v_{\varepsilon}(t))+J_{1/3}(v_{\varepsilon}(t)))^{2}).

(12.18)

According to (
4.10
), for small values of
|\hat{s}_{\varepsilon}|
, the right hand side of (
12.18
) is

O(\varepsilon^{1/3}|\hat{s}_{\varepsilon}||\hat{s}_{\varepsilon}|^{-2/3\cdot{3/2}}=O(\varepsilon^{1/3})
. According to (
4.9
), for
large values of
|\hat{s}_{\varepsilon}|
, the right hand side of (
12.18
) is
O(\varepsilon^{1/3}|\hat{s}_{\varepsilon}||\hat{s}_{\varepsilon}|^{-3/2})=O(\varepsilon^{1/3}|\hat{s}_{\varepsilon}|^{-1/2})
. This implies

|R_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{c}/\varepsilon)-R_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{\gamma}/\varepsilon)|=O(\varepsilon^{1/3}).

Together with the estimate

\hat{s}_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{c}/\varepsilon)=O(\varepsilon^{1/3}\ln\varepsilon)

and the asymptotic formula (
4.12
), this implies

|\hat{\zeta}_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{c}/\varepsilon)-\frac{1}{R_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{\gamma}/\varepsilon)}\frac{-2\pi i}{\Gamma^{2}(1/3)3^{1/6}}|=O(\varepsilon^{1/3}\ln\varepsilon).

\square

Proof of Lemma
7.6
.

Estimates (
7.17
) and (
7.18
) can be obtained in exactly the same way as the estimates in Lemma
7.5
. We omit the details.

Similarly to the proof of Lemma
7.5
, we can show that for the points on the curve

\varepsilon^{-1}\psi_{a}=-C_{a,0}
, we have
\hat{\sigma}_{\varepsilon}(\tau/\varepsilon)=\hat{\sigma}+O(\varepsilon^{1/3}\ln\varepsilon)
,
R_{\varepsilon}^{+}(\tau/\varepsilon)=R_{\varepsilon}^{+}(\tau_{c}/\varepsilon)+O(\varepsilon^{1/3})
. This implies

\hat{\chi}_{\varepsilon}(\tau/\varepsilon)=-i\sqrt{-\hat{\sigma}}\,\frac{J_{-2/3}(v)-R^{+}_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{c}/\varepsilon)J_{2/3}(v)}{R^{+}_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{c}/\varepsilon)J_{-1/3}(v)+J_{1/3}(v))}+O(\varepsilon^{1/3}\ln\varepsilon),\ v=\frac{2}{3}(-\hat{\sigma})^{3/2}.

(12.19)

From the expansion (
4.20
), we obtain

\hat{\chi}_{\varepsilon}(\tau/\varepsilon)=\sqrt{-\hat{\sigma}}\Bigl[1-2e^{-2iv}e^{\pi i/6}\frac{R^{+}_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{c}/\varepsilon)-e^{-2\pi i/3}}{R^{+}_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{c}/\varepsilon)-e^{2\pi i/3}}+O\Bigl(e^{-4|{\rm Im\,}v|}+\frac{1}{|v|}\Bigr)\Bigr]+O(\varepsilon^{1/3}\ln\varepsilon).

(12.20)

This implies, in view of (
7.14
),

c_{t,1}^{-1}e^{-C_{a,0}}-c_{t,2}\frac{1}{|v|}+O(\varepsilon^{1/3}\ln\varepsilon)<|\frac{\hat{\chi}_{\varepsilon}(\tau/\varepsilon)-\sqrt{-\hat{\sigma}}}{\sqrt{-\hat{\sigma}}}|<c_{t,1}e^{-C_{a,0}}+c_{t,2}\frac{1}{|v|}+O(\varepsilon^{1/3}\ln\varepsilon).

(12.21)

The equilibrium of the fast system on the considered curve in variables
\hat{\chi},\hat{\sigma}
is
\hat{\chi}_{e}
such that

|\hat{\chi}_{e}-\sqrt{-\hat{\sigma}}|=O(\varepsilon^{1/3})
.
In the original variables, we obtain

\displaystyle c_{m,26}^{-1}e^{-C_{a,0}}-c_{m,27}\frac{1}{|v|}+O(\varepsilon^{1/3}\ln\varepsilon)<|\frac{z(t)}{\sqrt{\sigma}}|<c_{m,26}e^{-C_{a,0}}+c_{m,27}\frac{1}{|v|}+O(\varepsilon^{1/3}\ln\varepsilon).

(12.22)

The estimate for
|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|
follows from the estimate for
|\kappa-\hat{\kappa}|
in Lemma
5.5
and from the estimate for
|\hat{\kappa}(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|
in Lemma
6.8
.

\square
