Proof of Lemma
7.8
.

Denote by
\Gamma^{C_{a,0}}
the arc of the curve
\operatorname{Re}\Psi={\rm const}
passing through the point
P_{1}
. Denote by

\tau_{+,C_{a,0}}
the point where this arc crosses the real axis
\operatorname{Im}\tau=0
. The distances from
P_{1}
to

\tau_{c}
and to the curve
\Gamma_{*,2}
are of order
\varepsilon^{2/3}
. Thus, we have

|\tau_{+,C_{a,0}}-\tau_{*}^{+}|/\sqrt{\varepsilon^{2/3}}=O(\varepsilon^{2/3})
(cf. Lemma
4.2
). Thus

|\tau_{+,C_{a,0}}-\tau_{*}^{+}|=O(\varepsilon)
.

Estimates in Lemma
6.1
and the estimates for
\Lambda_{1}
in Lemma
5.3
imply that
distance between the points where the curves
\Gamma^{C_{a,0}}
and
\Gamma^{C_{a,0},\varepsilon}
cross the real axis is

O(\varepsilon\ln\varepsilon)
. Thus,
|\tau_{+,C_{a,0},\varepsilon}-\tau_{*}^{+}|=O(\varepsilon\ln\varepsilon)
.

\square

Proof of Lemma
7.9
.

The possibility of continuing the solution into the domain
D_{3,1}
with the estimates given by Lemmas
6.8
,

13.8
follows directly from these lemmas.

The curve
\Gamma^{C_{a,o},\varepsilon}
with
\operatorname{Re}\tau\geq C_{a,1}\varepsilon^{2/3}
may consist of parts lying in the domains

D_{q,r}\setminus(D_{q,r,d}\cup\bar{D}_{q,r,d})
and
D_{q,r,d}\setminus\bar{D}_{q,r,d})
(cf. Lemma
6.10
).
The estimates
\eta=O(\varepsilon),w=O(\varepsilon)
on this curve in these domains follow from Lemma
6.8
.

According to (
5.7
), on the curve
\Gamma^{C_{a,o},\varepsilon}
we have

\displaystyle\dot{z}=\Lambda_{1}(\kappa)z+\varepsilon O(|z|^{2}d_{+}^{-3/2})+\varepsilon O(|\xi|_{*}^{2}d_{+}^{-1/2})+O(|\eta|(|\eta|+|w|))

\displaystyle+O(|\xi|_{*}^{3}d_{+}^{-1/2})+O(|z|^{5}d_{+}^{-3/2})+\varepsilon^{3}O(d_{+}^{-7/2}).

This can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\dot{z}=\Lambda_{1}(\kappa)z+\varepsilon O(|z|^{2}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2})+\varepsilon^{2}O(|z|\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2})

\displaystyle+O(\varepsilon|z|^{2}\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2})+O(|z|^{5}d_{+}^{-3/2})+\varepsilon^{3}O(\hat{d}_{+}^{-7/2})+O(\varepsilon^{2}).

Lemma
6.9
implies that on the curve
\Gamma^{C_{a,o},\varepsilon}
, for
\operatorname{Re}\tau\leq\operatorname{Re}Q_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{1}(\kappa(t))-\Lambda_{1}(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|=O(\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-5})+(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-1}|\ln(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2})|)\left(\varepsilon^{4/3}+\varepsilon^{5/3}d_{+}^{-2}+\varepsilon^{7/3}d_{+}^{-3}\right).

(We do not indicate the dependence on
C_{q}
here, since the value of
C_{q}
is already fixed.)

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\dot{z}=(\Lambda_{1}(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t))+\alpha)z+\beta,

\displaystyle\alpha=O(\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-5}+(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-1}|\ln(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2})|)\left(\varepsilon^{4/3}+\varepsilon^{5/3}d_{+}^{-2}+\varepsilon^{7/3}d_{+}^{-3}\right)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon|z|\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2}+\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon|z|\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}+|z|^{4}d_{+}^{-3/2}),

\displaystyle\beta=\varepsilon^{3}O(\hat{d}_{+}^{-7/2})+O(\varepsilon^{2}).

As
|z|=O(\varepsilon^{1/3})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\alpha=O(\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-5}+(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-1}|\ln(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2})|)\left(\varepsilon^{4/3}+\varepsilon^{5/3}d_{+}^{-2}+\varepsilon^{7/3}d_{+}^{-3}\right)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{4/3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2}+\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon^{4/3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon^{4/3}d_{+}^{-3/2}).

The integral of
|\alpha|
over the part of
\Gamma^{C_{a,0},\varepsilon}
with
\operatorname{Re}\tau_{c}+C_{a,1}\varepsilon^{2/3}\leq\operatorname{Re}\tau\leq\operatorname{Re}Q_{1}
does not exceed
c_{t,1}C_{a,1}^{-1/2}
.

7
7

7

The main contribution
\sim 1

comes from the terms
\varepsilon|z|\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2}
and
|z|^{4}d_{+}^{-3/2}
. The contribution of all other terms is
O(\varepsilon^{1/3}\ln\varepsilon)
.

The integral of
|\beta|
over the same part of
\Gamma^{C_{a,0},\varepsilon}
does not exceed

\varepsilon^{1/3}C_{a,1}^{-5/2}
.

We have
\hat{\sigma}_{1}=c_{m,29}C_{a,1}
,
c_{m,28}^{-1}\varepsilon^{1/3}\sqrt{|\hat{\sigma}_{1}|}e^{-C_{a,0}}<|z_{1}|<c_{m,28}\varepsilon^{1/3}\sqrt{|\hat{\sigma}_{1}|}e^{-C_{a,0}}
.

Already obtained estimates in this lemma imply that the change of
z
along the considered part of
\Gamma^{C_{a,o},\varepsilon}
does not exceed

c_{t,2}C_{a,1}^{-1/2}|z_{1}|+c_{t,3}\varepsilon^{1/3}C_{a,1}^{-5/2}
. We would like to choose
C_{a,1}
such that this change is smaller than
0.5|z_{1}|
. For this, it suffices to require

\varepsilon^{1/3}C_{a,1}^{-5/2}<c_{t,4}^{-1}\varepsilon^{1/3}\sqrt{|\hat{\sigma}|}e^{-C_{a,0}}
, which is satisfied if
C_{a,1}^{-5/2}<c_{t,4}^{-1}(c_{m,29}C_{a,1})^{1/2}e^{-C_{a,0}}
.
This, in turn, holds for
C_{a,1}>c_{m,30}e^{C_{a,0}/3}
. The value
C_{a,1}
should also satisfy the condition
C_{a,1}>c_{m,25}e^{2C_{a,0}/3}
introduced at the end of Section
7.4
.

\square

Proof of Lemma
7.10
.

The possibility of continuing the solution into the domain

D_{3,2}
with the estimates given by Lemma
6.8
follows directly from
Lemma
6.8
. In particular, in this domain we have

z(t)=O(\varepsilon^{1/3}),\ w(t)=O(\varepsilon^{1/3}),\ \eta(t)=O(\varepsilon),\ |\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|=O(\varepsilon).

According to (
5.7
), on the curve
\Gamma^{C_{a,o},\varepsilon}
in
D_{3,2}
we have

\displaystyle\dot{z}=\Lambda_{1}(\kappa)z+\varepsilon O(|z|^{2}d_{+}^{-3/2})+\varepsilon O(|\xi|_{*}^{2}d_{+}^{-1/2})+O(|\eta|(|\eta|+|w|))

\displaystyle+O(|\xi|_{*}^{3}d_{+}^{-1/2})+O(|z|^{5}d_{+}^{-3/2})+\varepsilon^{3}O(d_{+}^{-3}|\xi|)+\varepsilon^{3}O^{*}(d_{+}^{-7/2}),

This can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\dot{z}=\Lambda_{1}(\kappa)z+\varepsilon O(|z|^{2})+O(\varepsilon^{4/3}|z|)+O(\varepsilon^{4/3})

\displaystyle+O(\varepsilon^{1/3}|z|^{2})+O(|z|^{5})+O(\varepsilon^{3}|z|)+\varepsilon^{10/3}+O(\varepsilon^{3}).

This can further be rewritten as

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\dot{z}=(\Lambda_{1}(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t))+\alpha)z+\beta,

(12.26)

\displaystyle\alpha=O(\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{4/3}+\varepsilon^{4/3}+\varepsilon^{2/3}+\varepsilon^{4/3}+\varepsilon^{3})=O(\varepsilon^{2/3}),

\displaystyle\beta=O(\varepsilon^{4/3}+\varepsilon^{10/3}+\varepsilon^{3})=O(\varepsilon^{4/3}).

Denote by
P_{2}
the point of the curve
\Gamma^{C_{a,o},\varepsilon}
where
\operatorname{Re}P_{2}=\operatorname{Re}Q_{1}
. Let
z_{2}
be the value of
z(t)
at this point. According to Lemma
7.9
,
c_{t,1}^{-1}\varepsilon^{1/3}<|z_{2}|<c_{t,1}\varepsilon^{1/3}
. The change in
t
along the curve
\Gamma^{C_{a,o},\varepsilon}
from the point
P_{2}
till the real axis is
O(\ln\varepsilon)
. Then (
12.26
) implies that the change of

|z(t)|
along the curve
\Gamma^{C_{a,o},\varepsilon}
from the point
P_{2}
till the real axis is
O(\varepsilon\ln\varepsilon)
. Thus, at the point
\tau_{+,C_{a,0},\varepsilon}
of intersection of
\Gamma^{C_{a,o},\varepsilon}
with the real axis, we have
|z(t)|=|w(t)|>0.5c_{t,1}^{-1}\varepsilon^{1/3}
.

\square

Proof of Lemma
7.11
.

Estimates for the transformations in Lemmas
5.2
,
5.3
,
5.4
, and
5.5
show that the difference between the original and transformed variables at
\tau=\tau_{+,C_{a,0},\varepsilon}
is
O(\varepsilon^{2/3})
for
z,w,\eta
and
O(\varepsilon^{4/3})
for
\kappa
. Thus, in the original variables at
\tau=\tau_{+,C_{a,0},\varepsilon}
, we have

c_{t,1}^{-1}\varepsilon^{1/3}<|z(t)|=|w(t)|<c_{t,1}\varepsilon^{1/3},\ \eta(t)=O(\varepsilon^{2/3}),\ |\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|=O(\varepsilon).

According to Lemma
6.1
,
|\mathrm{K}(\varepsilon t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|=O(\varepsilon)
. Thus,
|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}(\varepsilon t)|=O(\varepsilon)
.

\square

Proof of Lemma
7.12
.

This is a standard situation of a fast departure from a non-degenerate linearly unstable equilibrium of the fast system when a phase point is initially not too close to this equilibrium. We include a proof here for completeness of the exposition.

Denote
t_{5}=\tau_{+,C_{a,0}}/\varepsilon
. For real
t\geq t_{5}
, we have

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\dot{z}=\lambda_{1}(\mathrm{K}(\varepsilon t))z+O(|z^{2}|+|z\eta|+|\eta|^{2}+|\kappa-\mathrm{K}(\varepsilon t)||z|+\varepsilon),

(12.27)

\displaystyle\dot{\eta}=B(\mathrm{K}(\varepsilon t))\eta+O(|z^{2}|+|z\eta|+|\eta|^{2}+|\kappa-\mathrm{K}(\varepsilon t)||\eta|+\varepsilon),

\displaystyle\dot{\kappa}=\varepsilon G(\kappa)+\varepsilon O(|z|+|\eta|+\varepsilon),

\displaystyle\dot{\mathrm{K}}(\varepsilon t)=\varepsilon G(\mathrm{K}(\varepsilon t)).

At
t=t_{5}
, we have

c_{t,1}^{-1}\varepsilon^{1/3}<|z(t_{5})|<c_{t,1}\varepsilon^{1/3},\ \eta(t_{5})=O(\varepsilon^{2/3}),\ |\kappa(t_{5})-\mathrm{K}(\varepsilon t_{5})|=O(\varepsilon).

We also have

\operatorname{Re}\lambda_{1,2}(\mathrm{K}(\varepsilon t_{5}))>c_{t,2}^{-1},\ \operatorname{Re}\lambda_{j}(\mathrm{K}(\varepsilon t_{5}))<-c_{t,3}^{-1},\ j=3,\ldots,n.

A
c_{t,4}^{-1}
-neighbourhood
U
of the point
(\operatorname{Re}z(t_{5}),\operatorname{Im}z(t_{5}),\eta(t_{5}),\kappa(t_{5}))
belongs to the domain
D
. Denote by
U_{1}
the
0.5c_{t,4}^{-1}
-neighbourhood of the point
(\operatorname{Re}z(t_{5}),\operatorname{Im}z(t_{5}),\eta(t_{5}))
in
x
-space.
Consider the time interval
[t_{5},t_{5}+\varepsilon^{-1/2}]
and its subinterval
[t_{5},t_{*}]
on which the point
(\operatorname{Re}z(t),\operatorname{Im}z(t),\eta(t))
remains inside
U_{1}
. The changes in
\kappa
and
\mathrm{K}
on this interval are
O(\varepsilon^{1/2})
. Thus, equations for
z,\eta
in system (
12.27
) take the form

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\dot{z}=\lambda_{1}(\mathrm{K}(\varepsilon t))z+O(|z^{2}|+|z\eta|+|\eta|^{2}+\varepsilon^{1/2}|z|+\varepsilon),

(12.28)

\displaystyle\dot{\eta}=B(\mathrm{K}(\varepsilon t))\eta+O(|z^{2}|+|z\eta|+|\eta|^{2}+\varepsilon^{1/2}|\eta|+\varepsilon).

On the considered time interval, we have
\operatorname{Re}\lambda_{1}(\mathrm{K}(\varepsilon t))>(3/4)c_{t,2}^{-1},\ \operatorname{Re}\lambda_{j}(\mathrm{K}(\varepsilon t))<-(3/4)c_{t,3}^{-1},\ j=3,\ldots,n
.
Thus,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}|z|^{2}=2(\operatorname{Re}\lambda_{1}(\mathrm{K}(\varepsilon t))|z|^{2}+O(|z^{3}|+|z|^{2}|\eta|+|z||\eta|^{2}+\varepsilon^{1/2}|z|^{2}+\varepsilon|z|)

(12.29)

\displaystyle\geq(3/2)c_{t,2}^{-1}|z|^{2}+O(|z^{3}|+|z|^{2}|\eta|+|z||\eta|^{2}+\varepsilon^{1/2}|z|^{2}+\varepsilon|z|).

According to
[
4
]
, Sect 13, the linear homogeneous system with the matrix
B(\mathrm{K}(\tau))
for frozen
\tau
admits a
quadratic Lyapunov function
W(\tau,\eta)
, whose derivative with respect to time is equal to
-(\eta\cdot\eta)
. Thus,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}W=-(\eta\cdot\eta)+O(\varepsilon|\eta|^{2}+|\eta|^{3}|+|\eta|^{2}|z|+|z|^{2}|\eta|+\varepsilon^{1/2}|\eta|^{2}+\varepsilon|\eta|).

(12.30)

Following the construction in the proof of the Chetaev instability theorem
[
6
]
, we consider the function

V(z,\eta,\tau)=|z|^{2}-W(\tau,\eta).

Then

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}V\geq(3/2)c_{t,2}^{-1}|z|^{2}+(\eta\cdot\eta)

(12.31)

\displaystyle+O(|z^{3}|+|z^{2}\eta|+|z||\eta|^{2}+\varepsilon^{1/2}|z|^{2}+|\eta^{3}|+|\eta|^{2}|z|+|z|^{2}|\eta|+\varepsilon^{1/2}|\eta|^{2})

\displaystyle+O(\varepsilon|z|+\varepsilon|\eta|)

\displaystyle\geq c_{t,6}^{-1}(|z|^{2}+(\eta\cdot\eta))+O(\varepsilon|z|+\varepsilon|\eta|)

provided that
c_{t,4}^{-1}
is sufficiently small.

We have
|z(t_{5})|>c_{t,1}^{-1}\varepsilon^{1/3}
. We chose
t_{*}
such that
|z(t)|>0.5c_{t,1}^{-1}\varepsilon^{1/3}
for

t_{5}\leq t\leq t_{*}
. Thus, on this time interval we have
|z|^{2}\gg\varepsilon|z|
.
For the values of
\eta
on the considered time interval we distinguish two cases a)
|\eta|\leq\varepsilon^{2/3}
, and b)
|\eta|>\varepsilon^{2/3}
.
In case a),
|\varepsilon\eta|\ll|z|^{2}
. In case b),
|\varepsilon\eta|\ll|\eta|^{2}
. Thus, in both cases

\frac{d}{dt}V\geq c_{t,7}^{-1}(|z|^{2}+|\eta|^{2})\geq c_{t,7}^{-1}V.

Then

V(z(t),\eta(t),\varepsilon t)\geq c_{t,8}^{-1}\varepsilon^{2/3}e^{c_{t,7}^{-1}t},

and thus

|z(t)|\geq c_{t,8}^{-1/2}\varepsilon^{1/3}e^{0.5c_{t,7}^{-1}t}

(12.32)

As
V
remains positive, we have
W(\eta(t),\varepsilon t)<|z|^{2}
, and hence
|\eta(t)|<c_{t,9}|z(t)|
.
This estimate together with (
12.32
) implies that there exists

t_{d}=t_{5}+O(|\ln\varepsilon|
and a constant
c_{t,9}
such that
z(t_{d})=c_{t,9}^{-1}
, and for
t_{5}\leq t\leq t_{d}
the point
(\operatorname{Re}z(t),\operatorname{Im}z(t),\eta(t))
does not leave
U_{1}
(i.e.
t_{d}<t_{*}
).
We have
\varepsilon t_{5}=\tau_{+,C_{a,0}}
, and
|\tau_{+,C_{a,0},\varepsilon}-\tau_{*}^{+}|=O(\varepsilon\ln\varepsilon)
. Therefore,
|\varepsilon t_{d}-\tau_{*}^{+}|=O(\varepsilon\ln\varepsilon)
.
Thus,
\tau_{d}=\varepsilon t_{d}
is the time moment claimed in Lemma
7.12
.

\square

13
Proofs of Lemmas from Section 11

Proof of lemma
11.1
.

Estimate
z(t)
. Each point in the time domain
S(T)
with
\operatorname{Im}t\geq 0
can be reached from
t_{4}
by first moving along the real axis and then along the curve
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
. Along the real axis, we have
z(t)=O(\varepsilon^{3})
. Let
\sigma
denote the arc length along the curve
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
. On this curve,
t=t(\sigma)
.
For the motion along the curve
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
in the domain
\operatorname{Re}\tau\geq-c_{l,1}^{-1}
, the equation for
z
in (
11.7
) takes the form

\frac{dz}{d\sigma}=i\omega(\varepsilon\sigma)z+\varepsilon^{3}\frac{dt}{d\sigma}O(\hat{d}_{+}^{-7/2}),

(13.1)

where
\omega=\Lambda_{1}(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon})({dt}/{d\sigma})i^{-1}
is a real-valued function bounded away from 0 by
c_{a,1}^{-1}{\hat{d}_{+}}^{\,1/2}
.

Estimates obtained from equation (
13.1
) imply that

|z(t)|<O(\varepsilon^{3})+\varepsilon^{2}O(\hat{d}_{+}^{-5/2}(\tau))<c_{r,5}\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+}^{-5/2}(\tau).

The last estimate can be improved.
Each point in the time domain
S(T)
can be reached from
t_{4}
by first moving along the curve
\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon}
and then vertically downward. For the downward motion along a vertical line
\operatorname{Re}t={\rm const}
, equation (
11.7
) takes the form

\frac{dz}{ds}=-i\Lambda_{1}(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t))z+\varepsilon^{3}O(\hat{d}_{+}^{-7/2}),\ s=-\operatorname{Im}t.

According to condition 6) in Section
2
, each vertical line
crosses the curves
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
transversally. Thus,
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}
decreases downward along the vertical line, and
\operatorname{Re}(-i\Lambda_{1}(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t))<-c_{a,2}^{-1}\hat{d}_{+}^{1/2}
. This implies that
|z(t)|
decreases for motion downward along any line
\operatorname{Re}t={\rm const}
while
|z(t)|>c_{a,3}^{-1}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4}
.
This, in particular, implies that at
0\leq\operatorname{Im}\tau\leq 2c_{l,1}^{-1}
we have
|z(t)|=O(\varepsilon^{3})
.

In a similar way, by considering motion vertically downward from the real axis, we obtain that
|z(t)|=O(\varepsilon^{3})
for
-2c_{l,1}^{-1}\leq\operatorname{Im}\tau\leq 0
, and
|z(t)|<c_{r,8}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{-}^{-3}
for
\operatorname{Im}\tau\leq c_{l,1}^{-1}
. This implies that
|w(t)|<c_{r,8}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}
for
\operatorname{Im}\tau\geq-c_{l,1}^{-1}

Estimate
\eta(t)
. For definiteness, consider the half-plane
\operatorname{Im}t\geq 0
. Equation for
\eta
in (
11.7
) has the form

\displaystyle\dot{\eta}=B(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon})\eta+\alpha,|\alpha|<2c_{r,2}\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}.

(13.2)

Along the real axis, we have
|\eta(t)|<c_{a,4}\varepsilon^{3}
. Each point in the half-plane
\operatorname{Im}t\geq 0
can be reached from the real axis
by first moving along the real axis and then along the curve

\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}(t)={\rm const}
. We introduce the arc length
\sigma
along this curve as a new time parameter. Thus
t=t(\sigma),\ |dt/d\sigma|=1
.

The equation for
\eta
on the curve
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
takes the form

\frac{d\eta}{d\sigma}=\frac{dt}{d\sigma}B(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon})\eta+\alpha,\ \alpha=\varepsilon^{3}\frac{dt}{d\sigma}\tilde{O}_{2}(d_{+}^{-3}),\ |\alpha|<2c_{r,2}\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}.

(13.3)

According to condition 5) in Section
2
, all eigenvalues of the matrix
({dt}/{d\sigma})B(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon})
have negative real parts.

According to
[
4
]
, Sect 13, the corresponding to (
13.3
) linear homogeneous system
(i.e. with
\alpha=0
)
for frozen
\tau
has a
quadratic Lyapunov function
W(\tau,\eta)
whose
\sigma
-derivative for frozen
\tau
is equal to
-(\eta\cdot\bar{\eta})
.

We have
c_{a,5}^{-1}|\eta|^{2}\leq W(\eta)\leq c_{a,5}|\eta|^{2}
. The derivative of
W
in the original system is

\frac{dW}{d\sigma}=-(\eta\cdot\bar{\eta})+O(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2})W_{1}(\eta)+W_{2}(\alpha,\eta)

with a hermitian quadratic form
W_{1}
and hermitian bilinear form
W_{2}
.

We have
|W_{2}(\alpha,\eta)|\leq c_{a,6}|\alpha||\eta|\leq c_{a,7}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}\sqrt{W}
. This implies

\frac{dW}{d\sigma}\leq-0.5c_{a,5}^{-1}W+c_{a,7}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}\sqrt{W}.

For the starting point on the real time axis, we consider two cases: (a)
W^{1/2}<4c_{a,5}c_{a,7}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}
, and (b)
W^{1/2}\geq 4c_{a,5}c_{a,7}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}
. For the case (a), inequality
W^{1/2}<4c_{a,5}c_{a,7}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}
will be satisfied up to the line
\operatorname{Re}\tau=\tau_{c}
. Indeed, starting from the moment, when this inequality is not satisfied, we should have
{dW}/{d\sigma}<0
, i.e.
W
decays, while
\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}
grows, which leads to a contradiction. For the case (b), starting from the real axis,
W
decays, while
\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}
grows. If
W^{1/2}\geq 4c_{a,5}c_{a,7}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}
for all time up to
\operatorname{Re}\tau=\tau_{c}
, then, because, initially,
W^{1/2}=O(\varepsilon^{3})
, we would have
W^{1/2}=O(\varepsilon^{3})
for all this time interval. Suppose now that there is a moment of time when
W^{1/2}=4c_{a,5}c_{a,7}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}
for the first time. From this time onward, we have
W^{1/2}\leq 4c_{a,5}c_{a,7}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}
, as in case (a). Thus, in both cases (a) and (b) we obtain
W^{1/2}=O(\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3})
. This implies that
|\eta|=O(\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3})
.

For
\dot{\kappa}
, we have

\displaystyle\dot{\kappa}=\varepsilon F(\kappa)+\varepsilon^{2}O(|z|^{2}d_{+}^{-2})+\varepsilon^{2}O(|\xi|_{*}^{2}d_{+}^{-3/2})

\displaystyle+\varepsilon O\left(|z|^{4}+|z|^{5}d_{+}^{-2}+|\xi|_{*}^{3}\right)+\varepsilon^{3}O(|z|d_{+}^{-3}+|\xi|_{*}d_{+}^{-3/2})+\varepsilon^{4}O(d_{+}^{-7/2}|\xi|),

\displaystyle F=g(X(\kappa),\kappa,0)+\varepsilon O_{3}(d_{+}^{-1}).

For terms in
\dot{\kappa}
, at
\operatorname{Im}\tau\geq-c_{l,1}^{-1}
, we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}O(|z|^{2}d_{+}^{-2})+\varepsilon^{2}O(|\xi|_{*}^{2}d_{+}^{-3/2})=O(\varepsilon^{2}(\varepsilon^{2}d_{+}^{-5/2})^{2}d_{+}^{-2})=O(\varepsilon^{6}d_{+}^{-7}),

(13.4)

\displaystyle\varepsilon O\left(|z|^{4}+|\xi|_{*}^{3}\right)=O(\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{2}d_{+}^{-5/2})^{4})=O(\varepsilon^{9}d_{+}^{-10}),

\displaystyle\varepsilon O(|z|^{5}d_{+}^{-2})=O(\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{2}d_{+}^{-5/2})^{5}d_{+}^{-2})=O(\varepsilon^{11}d_{+}^{-29/2}),

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{3}O(|z|d_{+}^{-3}+|\xi|_{*}d_{+}^{-3/2})=O(\varepsilon^{3}(\varepsilon^{2}d_{+}^{-5/2})d_{+}^{-3})=O(\varepsilon^{5}d_{+}^{-11/2}),

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{4}O(d_{+}^{-7/2}|\xi|)=O(\varepsilon^{4}d_{+}^{-7/2}(\varepsilon^{2}d_{+}^{-5/2})=O(\varepsilon^{6}d_{+}^{-6}).

We have

O(\varepsilon^{6}d_{+}^{-7})+O(\varepsilon^{9}d_{+}^{-10})+O(\varepsilon^{11}d_{+}^{-29/2})+O(\varepsilon^{5}d_{+}^{-11/2})=O(\varepsilon^{5}d_{+}^{-11/2}).

Thus,

\dot{\kappa}=\varepsilon F(\kappa)+O(\varepsilon^{5}{d}_{+}^{-11/2})=\varepsilon F(\kappa)+O(\varepsilon^{5}\hat{d}_{+}^{-11/2}).

Similarly for
\operatorname{Im}\tau\leq c_{l,1}^{-1}
.

Thus, in the considered domain,

|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|<c_{r,7}\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{\pm}^{-9/2}.

(This also absorbs the effect of the difference in the initial conditions
|\kappa(t_{4})-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t_{4})|=O(\varepsilon^{6}\ln\varepsilon)
).

\square

Proof of lemma
11.2
.

For
\operatorname{Im}\tau\geq-c_{l,1}^{-1}
, we have

\displaystyle\beta_{1}=\varepsilon O(|z|^{2}d_{+}^{-3/2})+\varepsilon O(|\xi|_{*}^{2}d_{+}^{-1/2})+O(|\eta|(|\eta|+|w|))

(13.5)

\displaystyle+O(|\xi|_{*}^{3}d_{+}^{-1/2})+O(|z|^{5}d_{+}^{-3/2})+\varepsilon^{3}O(d_{+}^{-3}|\xi|),

\displaystyle\beta_{2}=O(|\eta|^{2})+O(|\eta|(|z|+|w|))+O(|zw|)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon O((|z|^{2}+|w|^{2})d_{+}^{-1/2})+O(|\xi|_{*}^{3}d_{+}^{-1/2})+O(|z|^{4})+\varepsilon^{3}O(d_{+}^{-3}|\xi|),

\displaystyle\dot{\kappa}=\varepsilon F(\kappa)+\varepsilon^{2}O(|z|^{2}d_{+}^{-2})+\varepsilon^{2}O(|\xi|_{*}^{2}d_{+}^{-3/2})

\displaystyle+\varepsilon O\left(|z|^{4}+|z|^{5}d_{+}^{-2}+|\xi|_{*}^{3}\right)+\varepsilon^{3}O(|z|d_{+}^{-3}+|\xi|_{*}d_{+}^{-3/2})+\varepsilon^{4}O(d_{+}^{-7/2}|\xi|),

\displaystyle F=g(X(\kappa),\kappa,0)+O(\varepsilon d_{+}^{-1}).

Estimate terms in these relations using (
11.9
), (
11.10
).

For terms in
\beta_{1}
, provided that
d_{+}>c_{*}\varepsilon^{2/3}
with sufficiently large constant
c_{*}
, we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon O(|z|^{2}d_{+}^{-3/2})=O(\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{2}{d}_{+}^{-5/2})^{2}d_{+}^{-3/2})=O(\varepsilon^{5}d_{+}^{-13/2})<0.05c_{r,1}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-7/2},

(13.6)

\displaystyle\varepsilon O(|\xi|_{*}^{2}d_{+}^{-1/2})=O(\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{2}{d}_{+}^{-5/2}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-3})d_{+}^{-1/2})=O(\varepsilon^{6}d_{+}^{-6})<0.05c_{r,1}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-7/2},

\displaystyle O(|\eta|(|\eta|+|w|))=O((\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-3})^{2})=O(\varepsilon^{6}{d}_{+}^{-6})<0.05c_{r,1}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-7/2},

\displaystyle O(|\xi|_{*}^{3}d_{+}^{-1/2})=O(((\varepsilon^{2}{d}_{+}^{-5/2})^{2}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-3})d_{+}^{-1/2})=O(\varepsilon^{7}{d}_{+}^{-17/2})<0.05c_{r,1}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-7/2},

\displaystyle O(|z|^{5}d_{+}^{-3/2})=O((\varepsilon^{2}{d}_{+}^{-5/2})^{5}{d}_{+}^{-3/2})=O(\varepsilon^{10}{d}_{+}^{-14})<0.05c_{r,1}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-7/2},

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{3}O(d_{+}^{-3}|\xi|)=O(\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}(\varepsilon^{2}{d}_{+}^{-5/2}))=O(\varepsilon^{5}d_{+}^{-11/2})<0.05c_{r,1}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-7/2}.

For terms in
\beta_{2}
, provided that
d_{+}>c_{*}\varepsilon^{2/3}
with sufficiently large constant
c_{*}
, we have

\displaystyle O(|\eta|^{2})=O((\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-3})^{2})=O(\varepsilon^{6}{d}_{+}^{-6})<0.05c_{r,2}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-3}

(13.7)

\displaystyle O(|\eta z|)=O(\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-3}\varepsilon^{2}{d}_{+}^{-5/2})=O(\varepsilon^{5}{d}_{+}^{-11/2})<0.05c_{r,2}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-3},

\displaystyle O(|\eta w|)=O(\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-3}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-3})=O(\varepsilon^{6}{d}_{+}^{-6})<0.05c_{r,2}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-3},

\displaystyle O(|zw|)=O(\varepsilon^{2}{d}_{+}^{-5/2}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-3})=O(\varepsilon^{5}{d}_{+}^{-11/2})<0.05c_{r,2}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-3},

\displaystyle\varepsilon O(|z|^{2}){d}_{+}^{-1/2}=O(\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{2}{d}_{+}^{-5/2})^{2}{d}_{+}^{-1/2})=O(\varepsilon^{5}{d}_{+}^{-11/2})<0.05c_{r,2}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-3},

\displaystyle\varepsilon O(|w|^{2}){d}_{+}^{-1/2}=O(\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-3})^{2}d_{+}^{-1/2})=O(\varepsilon^{7}d_{+}^{-13/2})<0.05c_{r,2}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-3},

\displaystyle O(|\xi|^{3}_{*}{d}_{+}^{-1/2})=O((\varepsilon^{2}{d}_{+}^{-5/2})^{2}(\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-3}){d}_{+}^{-1/2})=O(\varepsilon^{7}{d}_{+}^{-17/2})<0.05c_{r,2}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-3}

\displaystyle O(|z|^{4})=O((\varepsilon^{2}{d}_{+}^{-5/2})^{4})=O(\varepsilon^{8}{d}_{+}^{-10})<0.05c_{r,2}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-3},

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{3}O(d_{+}^{-3}|\xi|)=O(\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}(\varepsilon^{2}{d}_{+}^{-5/2}))=O(\varepsilon^{5}d_{+}^{-11/2})<0.05c_{r,2}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-3}.

For
\operatorname{Im}\tau\leq c_{l,1}^{-1}
, we have

\beta_{4}=O(|\eta|(|\eta|+|w|))+\varepsilon O(|z||w|d_{-}^{-3/2})+\varepsilon O(|\xi|^{2}d_{-}^{-1/2})+O(|\xi|_{**}^{3}d_{-}^{-1/2})+O(|w|^{3})+\varepsilon^{3}O(d_{-}^{-3}|\xi|).

(13.8)

For terms in
\beta_{4}
, provided that
d_{-}>c_{*}\varepsilon^{2/3}
with sufficiently large constant
c_{*}
, we have

\displaystyle O(|\eta|(|\eta|+|w|))=O((\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{-}^{-3})(\varepsilon^{2}{d}_{-}^{-5/2}))=O(\varepsilon^{5}{d}_{-}^{-11/2})<0.05c_{r,4}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{-}^{-3},

(13.9)

\displaystyle\varepsilon O(|z||w|d_{-}^{-3/2})=O(\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{-}^{-3})(\varepsilon^{2}{d}_{-}^{-5/2})d_{-}^{-3/2})=O(\varepsilon^{6}d_{-}^{-7})<0.05c_{r,4}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{-}^{-3},

\displaystyle\varepsilon O(|\xi|^{2}d_{-}^{-1/2})=O(\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{2}{d}_{-}^{-5/2})^{2}{d}_{-}^{-1/2})=O(\varepsilon^{5}\ {d}_{-}^{-11/2})<0.05c_{r,4}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{-}^{-3},

\displaystyle O(|\xi|_{**}^{3}d_{-}^{-1/2})=O((\varepsilon^{2}{d}_{-}^{-5/2})^{2}(\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{-}^{-3})d_{-}^{-1/2})=O(\varepsilon^{7}{d}_{-}^{-17/2})<0.05c_{r,4}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{-}^{-3},

\displaystyle O(|w|^{3})=O((\varepsilon^{2}{d}_{-}^{-5/2})^{3})=O(\varepsilon^{6}{d}_{-}^{-15/2})<0.05c_{r,4}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{-}^{-3},

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{3}O(d_{-}^{-3}|\xi|)=O(\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-3}(\varepsilon^{2}{d}_{-}^{-5/2}))=O(\varepsilon^{5}d_{-}^{-11/2})<0.05c_{r,4}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{-}^{-3}.

Thus,

|\beta_{1}|<0.4\varepsilon^{3}c_{r,1}{d}_{+}^{-7/2},\ |\beta_{2}|<0.4\varepsilon^{3}c_{r,2}{d}_{\pm}^{-3},\ |\beta_{4}|<0.4\varepsilon^{3}c_{r,4}{d}_{-}^{-3}.

For
\kappa
, we have

|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|<c_{r,7}\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{\pm}^{-9/2}<\mu_{1}\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{\pm}^{-9/2}

(This also absorbs the effect of the difference in the initial conditions
|\kappa(t_{4})-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t_{4})|=O(\varepsilon^{6}\ln\varepsilon)
).

Then

|{d}_{\pm}(\kappa(t))-\hat{d}_{\pm}(\varepsilon t)|<c_{a,1}\varepsilon^{4}{d}_{\pm}^{-9/2}(\kappa(t))\ll{d}_{\pm}(\kappa(t)).

This implies

0.6\hat{d}_{\pm}(\varepsilon t)\leq d_{\pm}(\kappa(t))\leq 1.5\hat{d}_{\pm}(\varepsilon t).

If constant
c_{e,5}
is chosen sufficiently large, then
d_{\pm}>c_{*}\varepsilon^{2/3}
with a sufficiently large constant
c_{*}
. Then the above estimate imply that,
for any
\varepsilon T<\operatorname{Re}\tau_{c}
, the assumptions (
11.6
) are satisfied with a margin.

\square

Proof of Lemma
11.3
.

Denote by
\tau_{u}
the upper endpoint of the considered segment.
Denote

\tilde{d}_{+}(\tau)=b\cdot(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\tau)-\kappa_{c}),\ \tilde{d}_{u}=\tilde{d}_{+}(\tau_{u})=b\cdot(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{u})-\kappa_{c})
. Thus
\hat{d}_{+}(\tau)=|\tilde{d}_{+}(\tau)|,\ \hat{d}_{u}=|\tilde{d}_{u}|.

Then

|\tilde{d}_{u}-\tilde{d}_{+}(\tau)|=|b\cdot(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{u})-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\tau)|\leq c_{a,1}|\tau_{u}-\tau|.

Taking
|\tau_{u}-\tau|<0.5c_{a,1}^{-1}\hat{d}_{u}
, we obtain result of the Lemma with

c_{r,5}=2c_{a,1}
.

\square

Proof of Lemma
11.4
.

We should prove that

c_{e,12}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-1/2}|\ln(c_{e,11}^{-1}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})|<c_{r,5}^{-1}\hat{d}_{u},

which can be rewritten as

c_{r,5}c_{e,12}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-3/2}|\ln(c_{e,11}^{-1}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})|<1.

For
\varepsilon^{2/3}(C_{q,1}+o(1))\leq\hat{d}_{u}\leq c_{a,1}^{-1}
the left hand side of this inequality is a monotonic function of
\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-3/2}
. Its value is
maximal at

\hat{d}_{u}=\varepsilon^{2/3}(C_{q,1}+o(1))
. The inequality takes the form

c_{r,5}c_{e,12}(C_{q,1}+o(1))^{-3/2}|\ln(c_{e,11}^{-1}C_{q,1}+o(1))^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})|<1.

Since
C_{q,1}
grows linearly in the principal approximation with growth of
C_{q}
for large
C_{q}
, this inequality is satisfied for sufficiently large
C_{q}
.

\square

Proof of Lemma
11.5
.

Demonstrate closeness of tangent directions of curves
\tilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}=\tilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{q,r,d}
and
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
(Figure
10
).

Figure 10:
Curves
\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon}
,
\tilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{q,r,d}
and
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}

For a point
\tau_{u}\in\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon}
, we denote
\hat{d}_{u}=\hat{d}(\tau_{u}),\Lambda_{1,u}=\Lambda_{1}(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{u})),\\
\rho=-c_{e,12,1}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-1/2}\ln(c_{e,11,1}^{-1}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})
. Denote
\tau_{v}=\tau_{u}-i\rho\in\tilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}
.
Fix some
\tau_{u}=\tau_{u,*}\in\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon}
and the corresponding values

\hat{d}_{u}=\hat{d}_{u,*},\Lambda_{1,u}=\Lambda_{1,u,*},\rho=\rho_{*}
. Consider the level curve
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}(\tau)={\rm const}
passing through
the point
\tau_{v,*}=\tau_{u,*}-i\rho_{*}
. Denote
\Lambda_{1,v,*}=\Lambda_{1}(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\tau_{v,*}))
.

The tangent direction to
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
at the point
\tau_{v,*}
is given by the complex number

i\bar{\Lambda}_{1,v,*}/|\Lambda_{1,v,*}|
.

For points on
\tilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}
, we have
\tau_{v}=\tau_{u}-i\rho
. Let
\sigma
and
\tilde{\sigma}
denote the arc lengths along
\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon}
and
\tilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}
, respectively. We have

\left(\frac{d\tilde{\sigma}}{d\sigma}\right)_{\tau_{u}=\tau_{u,*}}=|\frac{d\tau_{v}}{d\tau_{u}}|_{\tau_{u}=\tau_{u,*}}=1+O\left(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u,*}^{-3/2}\ln(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u,*}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})\right).

The tangent direction to
\tilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}
at the point
\tau_{v,*}
is given by the complex number

\displaystyle\left(\frac{d\tau_{v}}{d\tilde{\sigma}}\right)_{\tau_{u}=\tau_{u,*}}=\left(\frac{d\tau_{v}}{d\sigma}\frac{d\sigma}{d\tilde{\sigma}}\right)_{\tau_{u}=\tau_{u,*}}=\left(\frac{d\tau_{u}}{d\sigma}-i\frac{d\rho}{d\hat{d}_{u}}\frac{d\hat{d}_{u}}{d\sigma}\right)_{\tau_{u}=\tau_{u,*}}\left(1+O\left(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u,*}^{-3/2}\ln(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u,*}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})\right)\right)

\displaystyle=i\frac{\bar{\Lambda}_{1,u,*}}{|\Lambda_{1,u,*}|}+O\left(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u,*}^{-3/2}\ln(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u,*}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})\right)=i\frac{\bar{\Lambda}_{1,v,*}}{|\Lambda_{1,v,*}|}+O(\hat{d}_{u,*}^{-1}\rho_{*})+O\left(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u,*}^{-3/2}\ln(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u,*}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})\right)

\displaystyle=i\frac{\bar{\Lambda}_{1,v,*}}{|\Lambda_{1,v,*}|}+O\left(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u,*}^{-3/2}\ln(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u,*}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})\right).

Since
\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u,*}^{-3/2}<c_{b,1}C_{q}^{-3/2}
, the angle between the tangent directions to the curves
\tilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}
and
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
can be made arbitrary small by choosing
C_{q}
sufficiently large.

The same holds for the curve
\Gamma^{\prime}_{q,r,d}
.

\square

Proof of Lemma
11.6
.

Estimate
z(t)
on
\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon}
. Let
\sigma
denote the arc length along the curve
\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon}
. On this curve,
t=t(\sigma)
.

Lemma 13.1

For motion along
\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon}
, the equation for
z
in (
11.31
) takes the form

\frac{dz}{d\sigma}=i\omega(\varepsilon\sigma)z+\frac{dt}{d\sigma}\alpha_{1},

(13.10)

where

\omega=\Lambda_{1}(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon})({dt}/{d\sigma})i^{-1}
is a real-valued function bounded away from 0 by
c_{a,1}^{-1}{\hat{d}_{+}}^{\,1/2}
,
and

\displaystyle|\alpha_{1}|

\displaystyle<c_{a,2}(|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}(\varepsilon t)|d_{+}^{-1/2}|z|+\varepsilon|z|^{2}d_{+}^{-3/2}+\varepsilon|z|(\varepsilon^{17/6}d_{+,q}^{-3})d_{+}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{17/6}d_{+,q}^{-3})^{2}d_{+}^{-1/2}

(13.11)

\displaystyle+(\varepsilon^{17/6}d_{+,q}^{-3})^{2}+|z|^{2}(\varepsilon^{17/6}d_{+,q}^{-3})d_{+}^{-1/2}+|z|(\varepsilon^{17/6}d_{+,q}^{-3})^{2}d_{+}^{-1/2}+(\varepsilon^{17/6}d_{+,q}^{-3})^{3}d_{+}^{-1/2}

\displaystyle+|z|^{5}d_{+}^{-3/2}+\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-7/2}.

For the proof, one only needs to substitute the estimates from(
11.25
) into the first equation in (
5.7
).
This lemma implies the following estimate.

Lemma 13.2

If
\tau\in S(T)\cap\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon}
, then

|z(t)|<c_{a,3}\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2},\quad|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(t)|<c_{a,4}\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-9/2}
.

Lemma 13.3

If
\tau\in S(T)\cap D_{q,r,d}
, then

|z(t)|<c_{r,6}\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2},\ |\eta(t)|<c_{r,7}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3}
,

additionally, if

\tau\in S(T)\cap(D_{q,r,d}\setminus\tilde{D}_{q,r,d})
, then

|z(t)|<c_{r,9}(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4}),\ |\eta(t)|<c_{r,10}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3},

additionally, if
\tau\in S(T)\cap\Gamma_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
, then

|z(t)|<c_{r,12}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4}
.

To estimate
z(t)
in
S(T)\setminus(D_{q,r,d}\cup\bar{D}_{q,r,d})
, we use equation (
11.31
) with initial data on
\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon,d}^{\prime}
, and then equation (
11.32
) for motion vertically down along the lines
\operatorname{Re}\tau={\rm const}
.

Lemma 13.4

In
S(T)\setminus(D_{q,r,d}\cup\bar{D}_{q,r,d})
, we have

\displaystyle|z(t)|<c_{r,15}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4}\ \mbox{\rm if}\ \operatorname{Im}\tau\geq-c_{l,1}^{-1},

(13.12)

\displaystyle|z(t)|<c_{r,16}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{-}^{-3}\ \mbox{\rm if}\ \operatorname{Im}\tau\leq c_{l,1}^{-1}.

Corollary 13.1

The last relation implies

\displaystyle|w(t)|<c_{r,16}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}\ \mbox{\rm if}\ \operatorname{Im}\tau\geq-c_{l,1}^{-1},\tau\notin D_{q,r,d}.

(13.13)

To estimate
\eta(t)
in
S(T)
, we use equation (
11.31
) for
\operatorname{Im}\tau\geq-c_{l,1}^{-1}
, and the corresponding equation with
d_{-}
instead of
d_{+}
for
\operatorname{Im}\tau\leq-c_{l,1}^{-1}
, with initial data given by Lemma
6.6
. By considering motion along the curves
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
and their conjugate, and taking into account Lemma
15.1
, we obtain the following estimate.

Lemma 13.5

In
S(T)\setminus(D_{q,r,d}\cup\bar{D}_{q,r,d})
:

\displaystyle|\eta(t)|<c_{r,16}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}\ \mbox{\rm if}\ \operatorname{Im}\tau\geq-c_{l,1}^{-1}

(13.14)

\displaystyle|\eta(t)|<c_{r,16}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{-}^{-3}\ \mbox{\rm if}\ \operatorname{Im}\tau\leq c_{l,1}^{-1}.

Now we obtain estimates for
\kappa(t)
.

Lemma 13.6

In part of
S(T)
covered by the curves
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
passing through
D_{q,r,d}
, we have

|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|<c_{r,8}\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-9/2}.

(13.15)

According to Lemma
6.6
, on the real axis for
\operatorname{Re}\tau\leq\operatorname{Re}\tau_{c}
, we have

|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|=O(\varepsilon^{4})
. Using all above estimates, equations
11.21
for
\operatorname{Im}\tau\geq-c_{l,1}^{-1}
and analogous equations for
\operatorname{Im}\tau\leq c_{l,1}^{-1}
, in
S(T)\setminus(D_{q,r,d}\cup\bar{D}_{q,r,d})
we obtain

\displaystyle|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|<c_{r,17}\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{\pm}^{-9/2}

(13.16)

(‘‘+’’ for
\operatorname{Im}\tau\geq-c_{l,1}^{-1}
, ‘‘-’’ for
\operatorname{Im}\tau\leq c_{l,1}^{-1}
).

Lemma 13.7

For
\tau\in(\bar{D}_{q,r,d}\setminus D_{q,r,d})\cap S(T)
, we have

|z(t)|<c_{r,16}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{-,q}^{-3}.

(13.17)

For
\tau\in(\bar{D}_{q,r,d}\setminus\overline{\tilde{D}}_{q,r,d})\cap S(T)
, we have

|z(t)|<c_{r,16}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{-}^{-3}.

(13.18)

This lemma implies that
|w(t)|<c_{r,16}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}
for
\tau\in(D_{q,r,d}\setminus{\tilde{D}}_{q,r,d})\cap S(T)
.

It remains to obtain estimate of
|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|
in
D^{\prime}_{q,r,d}\setminus\tilde{D}_{q,r,d}
.

Lemma 13.8

If
\tau\in D^{\prime}_{q,r,d}\setminus\tilde{D}_{q,r,d}
,
then

|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|<c_{r,11}\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-9/2}.

(13.19)

This completes the proof of Lemma
11.6
.

\square

Proof of Lemma
11.7
.

The required properties for the domain
S(T)\cap D_{q,r,d}
are satisfied due to Lemma
11.6
.

The proof for the remaining part of
S(T)
follows from estimates in Lemma (
11.6
) completely analogously to the proof of Lemma
11.2
, and we omit it. On should take into account that
\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-4}<\varepsilon^{2}d_{+}^{-5/2}
, if
C_{q}
is sufficiently large.

\square

14
Proofs of Lemmas from Section 12

Proof of Lemma
12.1
.

We have

\displaystyle x=X(\kappa(t))+C(\kappa(t))\xi(t),\quad x=X(\kappa_{c})+C(\kappa_{c})\xi_{sm}(t),

(14.1)

\displaystyle z(t)=\xi_{1}(t)+i\xi_{2}(t),w(t)=\xi_{1}(t)-i\xi_{2}(t),

\displaystyle z_{sm}(t)=\xi_{1,sm}(t)+i\xi_{2,sm}(t),w_{sm}(t)=\xi_{1,sm}(t)-i\xi_{2,sm}(t).

Here the value
t
is taken on the lower boundary of the triangle
D_{\triangle}
, and we denote the first two components of the vectors
\xi(t)
and
\xi_{sm}(t)
by

\xi_{1}(t),\xi_{2}(t)
and
\xi_{1,sm}(t),\xi_{2,sm}(t)
, respectively.
Thus,

\displaystyle\xi_{sm}(t)=C^{-1}(\kappa_{c})(X(\kappa(t))-X(\kappa_{c}))+C^{-1}(\kappa_{c})C(\kappa(t))\xi(t).

(14.2)

We know that
\kappa(t)=\kappa_{c}+O(\varepsilon^{2/3})
. This implies that
C(\kappa(t))=C(\kappa_{c})+O(\varepsilon^{1/3})
and

C^{-1}(\kappa_{c})C(\kappa(t))=I+O(\varepsilon^{1/3})
, where
I
is the unit matrix.

Moreover,
X(\kappa(t))=X(\kappa_{c})+O(\varepsilon^{1/3})
, and

C^{-1}(\kappa_{c})(X(\kappa(t))-X(\kappa_{c}))=\begin{pmatrix}(u+v)/2\\
-i(u-v)/2\\
O(\varepsilon^{2/3})\end{pmatrix},u=O(\varepsilon^{1/3}),\ v=O(\varepsilon^{2/3}).

This is because the matrix
C^{-1}(\kappa_{c})
transforms
x-x_{c}
into a coordinate system in which the matrix
A_{c}
takes the block-diagonal form described in Lemma
5.2
.

Thus, we obtain estimate (
12.11
) of the lemma. In a similar way, we obtain estimate (
12.12
) of the lemma.

We also have

\displaystyle\xi_{sm}(t)=C^{-1}(\kappa_{c})(X(\kappa(t))-X(\kappa_{c}))+C^{-1}(\kappa_{c})C(\kappa(t))\xi(t),

(14.3)

\displaystyle\xi_{ms}(\bar{t})=C^{-1}(\bar{\kappa}_{c})(X(\kappa(\bar{t}))-X(\bar{\kappa}_{c}))+C^{-1}(\bar{\kappa}_{c})C(\kappa(\bar{t}))\xi(\bar{t}).

Relations

\displaystyle X(\bar{\kappa}_{c})=\bar{X}(\kappa_{c}),\ C(\bar{\kappa}_{c})=\bar{C}(\kappa_{c}),\ \kappa(\bar{t})=\bar{\kappa}(t),

(14.4)

\displaystyle X(\kappa(\bar{t}))=\bar{X}(\kappa(t)),\ C(\kappa(\bar{t}))=\bar{C}(\kappa(t)),\ \xi((\bar{t}))=\bar{\xi}((t))

imply that
\xi_{ms}(\bar{t})=\bar{\xi}_{sm}(t)
. This, in turn, implies that

z_{ms}(\bar{t})=\bar{w}_{sm}(t),\ w_{ms}(\bar{t})=\bar{z}_{sm}(t),\eta_{ms}(\bar{t})=\bar{\eta}_{sm}(t).

\square

15
Proofs of Lemmas from Section 13

Proof of Lemma
13.2
.

Denote
t_{q}=\tau_{q}/\varepsilon
. According to Lemma
6.6
, we have

|z(t_{q})|<c_{e,7}\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2},\quad|\kappa(t_{q})-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t_{q})|<c_{e,6}\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-9/2}.

On the curve
\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon}
, we have

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\dot{z}=\Lambda_{1}(\kappa)z+\varepsilon O(|z|^{2}d_{+}^{-3/2})+\varepsilon O(|\xi|_{*}^{2}d_{+}^{-1/2})+O(|\eta|(|\eta|+|w|)

(15.1)

\displaystyle+O(|\xi|_{*}^{3}d_{+}^{-1/2})+O(|z|^{5}d_{+}^{-3/2})+\varepsilon^{3}O(d_{+}^{-7/2}),

\displaystyle\dot{\kappa}=\varepsilon F(\kappa)+\varepsilon^{2}O(|z|^{2}d_{+}^{-2})+\varepsilon^{2}O(|\xi|_{*}^{2}d_{+}^{-3/2})

\displaystyle+\varepsilon O\left(|z|^{4}+|z|^{5}d_{+}^{-2}+|\xi|_{*}^{3}\right)+\varepsilon^{3}O(|z|d_{+}^{-3}+|\xi|_{*}d_{+}^{-3/2})+\varepsilon^{4}O(d_{+}^{-7/2}|\xi|).

Denote
z_{d}=\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2}<c_{b,1}\varepsilon^{1/3}C_{q}^{-5/2},\ \kappa_{d}=\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-9/2}<c_{b,2}\varepsilon C_{q}^{-9/2}
.
Denote by
z_{m}
and
\kappa_{m}
the suprema of the quantities
|z(t)|
and
|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|
on
\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon}
over the time interval from
t_{q}
to some
t_{m}
in
S(T)
. Assume that on this time interval
z_{m}<\beta z_{d}
, where
\beta
is a positive constant to be determined later. Note that this inequality with
\beta\geq c_{e,7}
is certainly satisfied for
t_{m}
sufficiently close to
t_{q}
.
Equations (
15.1
) imply, for the time interval from
t_{q}
to
t_{m}
,

\displaystyle|z(t)|

\displaystyle\leq z_{m}<c_{e,7}\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2}+c_{b,3}\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2}+c_{b,4}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\kappa_{m}z_{m}

(15.2)

\displaystyle+c_{b,5}\left(z_{m}^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-1/2}+z_{m}\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\varepsilon^{17/3}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-6}\frac{z_{m}}{z_{d}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}z_{m}^{2}\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}z_{m}^{5}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-1/2}\right).

The first, second, and third terms in the right hand side of this inequality appear, respectively, due to initial condition for
z
at
t=t_{q}
, the last term in equation for
\dot{z}
in (
15.1
), and the term
\Lambda_{1}(\kappa)z
in (
15.1
).
Equation (
15.2
) implies that

\displaystyle z_{m}

\displaystyle\left(1-c_{b,6}\left(z_{m}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3}+\varepsilon^{8/3}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-7/2}+z_{m}\varepsilon^{11/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}z_{m}^{4}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-1/2}\right)\right)

(15.3)

\displaystyle<c_{b,7}(z_{d}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\kappa_{m}z_{m}).

If
\beta<c_{b,8}C_{q}^{21/8}
, then on the time interval
[t_{q},t_{m})
we have

c_{b,6}\left(z_{m}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3}+\varepsilon^{14/3}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-6}+z_{m}\varepsilon^{11/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}z_{m}^{4}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-1/2}\right)<1/2.

(15.4)

Indeed, consider the largest terms in (
15.4
):

\displaystyle z_{m}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-1/2}<\beta z_{d}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-1/2}<\beta c_{b,3}\varepsilon^{1/3}C_{q}^{-5/2}(\varepsilon^{2/3}C_{q})^{-1/2}=\beta c_{b,9}C_{q}^{-3}<0.1(c_{b,6})^{-1},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}z_{m}^{4}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-1/2}<\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(\beta z_{d})^{4}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-1/2}<\beta^{4}c_{b,10}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon^{1/3}C_{q}^{-5/2})^{4}(\varepsilon^{2/3}C_{q})^{-1/2}=\beta^{4}c_{b,10}C_{q}^{-21/2}<0.1(c_{b,6})^{-1},

provided that
\beta<c_{b,8}C_{q}^{21/8}
. Other terms in (
15.4
) are smaller and can be estimated using the bound
|\xi|<c_{t,5}\varepsilon^{1/3}
.

Thus, we have

z_{m}<2c_{b,7}(z_{d}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\kappa_{m}z_{m}).

(15.5)

The second equation (
15.1
) implies that

\displaystyle|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|\leq\kappa_{m}<c_{b,11}\kappa_{d}+c_{b,12}z_{m}\left(\varepsilon z_{m}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-1}+\varepsilon^{23/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-7/2}+z_{m}^{3}+z_{m}^{4}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-1}\right.

(15.6)

\displaystyle\left.+z_{m}\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3}+\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+q}^{-2}+\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-1}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2}\right).

One can check that if
\beta<c_{b,13}C_{q}^{3/2}
, then on the time interval
[t_{q},t_{m})
we have

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\left(\varepsilon z_{m}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-1}+\varepsilon^{23/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-7/2}+z_{m}^{3}+z_{m}^{4}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-1}\right.

(15.7)

\displaystyle\left.+z_{m}\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3}+\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+q}^{-2}+\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-1}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2}\right)<\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-2}.

Indeed, consider the largest terms in (
15.7
) divided by
\varepsilon^{2}d_{+,q}^{-2}
:

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon z_{m}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-1}}{\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-2}}<\frac{\beta}{\varepsilon}z_{d}\hat{d}_{+,q}<c_{b,14}\frac{\beta}{\varepsilon}\varepsilon^{1/3}C_{q}^{-5/2}\varepsilon^{2/3}C_{q}=c_{b,14}\beta C_{q}^{-3/2}<0.1,

\displaystyle\frac{z_{m}^{4}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-1}}{\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-2}}<\frac{\beta^{4}}{\varepsilon^{2}}z_{d}^{4}\hat{d}_{+,q}<c_{a,6}\frac{\beta^{4}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\varepsilon^{4/3}C_{q}^{-10}\varepsilon^{2/3}C_{q}=c_{b,15}\beta^{4}C_{q}^{-9}<0.1,

provided that
\beta<c_{b,13}C_{q}^{3/2}
. Other terms in (
15.7
) are smaller and can be estimated using the bound
|\xi|<c_{t,5}\varepsilon^{1/3}
.

Thus, we have

\kappa_{m}<c_{b,11}\kappa_{d}+c_{b,12}z_{m}\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-2}.

(15.8)

From (
15.5
) and (
15.8
), we obtain

\displaystyle

\displaystyle|z(t)|\leq z_{m}<c_{b,16}(z_{d}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\kappa_{m}z_{m}),

(15.9)

\displaystyle|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|\leq\kappa_{m}<c_{b,17}(\kappa_{d}+\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-2}z_{m}).

Substitute the right inequality for
\kappa_{m}
in (
15.9
) to the inequality for
z_{m}
. We obtain

z_{m}<c_{b,16}\left(z_{d}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}c_{b,17}\kappa_{d}z_{m}+c_{b,17}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-2}z_{m}^{2}\right).

(15.10)

Consider the equality corresponding to this inequality:

\rho=c_{b,16}\left(z_{d}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}c_{b,17}\kappa_{d}\rho+c_{b,17}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-2}\rho^{2}\right).

Denote by
\rho_{1}
the smaller root of this equation.
Calculate
\rho_{1}
. We have

c_{b,18}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-2}\rho^{2}-(1-c_{b,18}\kappa_{d}/\varepsilon)\rho+c_{b,16}z_{d}=0,

where
c_{b,18}=c_{b,16}c_{b,17}
. Thus,

\rho_{1}=\frac{1}{2c_{b,18}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-2}}\left((1-c_{b,18}\kappa_{d}/\varepsilon)-\sqrt{(1-c_{b,18}\kappa_{d}/\varepsilon)^{2}-4c_{b,18}c_{b,16}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-2}z_{d}}\right).

It is convenient to rewrite this as

\rho_{1}=\frac{2c_{b,16}z_{d}}{\left((1-c_{b,18}\kappa_{d}/\varepsilon)+\sqrt{(1-c_{b,3}\kappa_{d}/\varepsilon)^{2}-4c_{b,18}c_{b,16}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-2}z_{d}}\right)}.

For sufficiently large
C_{q}
, we have
\rho_{1}<c_{b,19}z_{d}
.

We have
|z(t_{q})|<\rho_{1}
. Assume that at some moment of time
t_{*}
we have
|z(t_{*})|=\rho_{1}
for the first time. Then for supremum of
|z(t)|
on the time interval
from
t_{q}
to
t_{*}
we have
z_{m}=\rho_{1}
. Thus,

z_{m}=c_{b,16}\left(z_{d}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}c_{b,2}\kappa_{d}z_{m}+c_{b,2}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-2}z_{m}^{2}\right),

which contradicts (
15.10
). Thus,
z_{m}<\rho_{1}<c_{b,19}z_{d}
.

Taking
\beta=c_{b,19}
, we obtain that the assumption
z_{m}<\beta z_{d}
is satisfied on
\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon}
in
S(T)
, provided that
C_{q}
is sufficiently large.

Then

\kappa_{m}<c_{b,17}(\kappa_{d}+\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-2}\rho_{1})\leq c_{b,20}(\kappa_{d}+\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-2}z_{d})\leq c_{b,21}(\kappa_{d}+\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-2}\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2})<c_{b,7}\kappa_{d}.

Thus, on
\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon}
in
S(T)
,

|z(t)|<c_{a,3}\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2},\quad|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(t)|<c_{a,4}\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-9/2}.

\square

Proof of Lemma
13.3

The plan is to estimate
z(t)
, use this to estimate
\eta(t)
, and then use this to improve the estimate of
z(t)
.

Each point in the time domain
D_{q,r,d}
can be reached from
\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon}
by moving vertically downward.
Consider downward motion along a vertical line
\operatorname{Re}t={\rm const}
, while the relation

|z(t|<2c_{a,3}\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2}

(15.11)

is satisfied. Here
c_{a,3}
is the constant from Lemma
13.2
.
For this motion, the equation for
z
in (
5.7
) takes the form

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\frac{dz}{ds}=\left(-i\Lambda_{1}(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t))+\alpha_{2}\right)z+\alpha_{3}+\varepsilon^{3}(-i)O_{1}({d}_{+}^{-7/2}),\quad s=-\operatorname{Im}t,

(15.12)

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}=\mu_{1}\varepsilon^{4}O(\hat{d}_{+}^{-5})+\varepsilon O((\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2})\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2})+\varepsilon O((\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3})\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2})

\displaystyle+O((\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2})(\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3})\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2})+O((\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3})^{2}\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2})+O((\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2})^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2})+\varepsilon^{3}O(\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}),

\displaystyle\alpha_{3}=\varepsilon O((\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3})^{2}\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2})+O((\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+.q}^{-3})^{2})+O((\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3})^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2})+\varepsilon^{3}O((\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3})\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}).

(We used (
11.25
) here.)
According to condition 6) in Section
2
, each vertical line
crosses the curve
\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon,d}
and all curves

\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
transversally, if
c_{e,10}
is sufficiently large. Thus,
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}
decreases downward along these lines, and
\operatorname{Re}(-i\Lambda_{1}(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t))<-c_{b,1}^{-1}\hat{d}_{+}^{1/2}
.
We also have

|\alpha_{2}|<c_{b,2,1}C_{q}^{-9/2}\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2},\ |\alpha_{3}|=O((\varepsilon^{17/6}d_{+.q}^{-3})^{2})<c_{b,2,2}\varepsilon^{5/3}C_{q}^{-6}.

This estimate for
\alpha_{2}
originates from the term
\varepsilon O((\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2})\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2})
:

\frac{\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2})}{\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}}=\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2}\hat{d}_{+}^{-2}=O(\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-9/2})=O(C_{q}^{-9/2}).

Other terms in
\alpha_{2}
are smaller than this one.

Let us use notation
\hat{d}_{u}
from Lemma
11.3
. Using this lemma, on the segment of the considered line with length less than
c_{r,5}^{-1}\hat{d}_{u}
, and for sufficiently large
C_{q}
, we obtain the estimates

\operatorname{Re}\left(-i\Lambda_{1}(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)+\alpha_{2}\right)<-c_{b,3}^{-1}\hat{d_{u}}^{1/2},\quad|\alpha_{3}+\varepsilon^{3}(-i)O_{1}({d}_{+}^{-7/2})|<c_{b,4}(\varepsilon^{5/3}C_{q}^{-6}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{u}^{-7/2}).

Consider the auxiliary equation (
17.1
), and apply Lemma
17.1
for the case

\nu=c_{b,3}^{-1}\hat{d_{u}}^{1/2},\ \alpha_{a}=c_{b,4}(\varepsilon^{5/3}C_{q}^{-6}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{u}^{-7/2}),\ u(s_{0})=c_{a,3}\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2}.

(15.13)

For sufficiently large
C_{q}
, we have
\alpha_{a}/\nu<u(s_{0})
. Indeed,

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{a}}{\nu u(s_{0})}=O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{5/3}C_{q}^{-6}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{u}^{-7/2}}{\hat{d_{u}}^{1/2}\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2}}\right)=O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{5/3}C_{q}^{-6}}{\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-2}}+\frac{\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{u}^{-4}}{\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2}}\right)=O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{5/3}C_{q}^{-6}}{\varepsilon^{2/3}C_{q}^{-2}}+\frac{\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-4}}{\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2}}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\varepsilon C_{q}^{-4}+\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3/2}\right)=O(C_{q}^{-3/2})<1.

Solution
u(t)
of equation (
17.1
) can be used now to estimate
|z(t)|
in (
15.12
) on the vertical line. We take
s_{0}
as the value of
s
at the intersection of the vertical line with the curve
\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon}
. According to Lemma

17.2
, for
s>s_{0}
, while condition (
15.11
) is satisfied, we have

|z(t)|<u(s)
.
In particular, this implies that condition (
15.11
), namely
|z(t|<2c_{a,3}\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2}
, can not be violated in
D_{q,r,d}\cap S(T)
. Thus, in Lemma
13.3
we can take
c_{r,6}=c_{a,3}
.

Denote by
\tilde{D}_{q,r,d}
the part of
D_{q,r,d}
covered by vertical segments of length less than or equal to
(\varepsilon/\nu)|\ln(\alpha_{a}/(\nu u(s_{0}))|

down from all points
\tau_{u}\in\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon}
(later will use
c_{11},c_{12}
such that
\tilde{D}_{q,r,d}\subset D_{q,r,d}
). Denote by
\tilde{\Gamma}_{q,r,d}
the lower boundary of
\tilde{D}_{q,r,d}
. Denote by
\tilde{\Gamma}_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
the part of
\tilde{\Gamma}_{q,r,d}
consisting of points for which the lengths of the considered vertical segments are
(\varepsilon/\nu)|\ln(\alpha_{a}/(\nu u(s_{0}))|
. According to Lemmas
17.1
and
17.2
, in the whole
D_{q,r,d}
, we have
|z(t)|<c_{a,3}\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2}
, while on
\tilde{\Gamma}_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
and in
D_{q,r,d}\setminus\tilde{D}_{q,r,d}
we have

|z(t)|<2\alpha_{a}/\nu<c_{b,5}(\varepsilon^{5/3}C_{q}^{-6}\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4}).

(15.14)

Obtain estimate for
\eta(t)
in
S(T)\cap D_{q,r,d}
.
In this domain, the equation for
\eta
in (
11.31
) takes the form

\displaystyle\dot{\eta}

\displaystyle=\left(B(\kappa)+O(|\eta|)+O(|z|+|w|)\right)\eta

\displaystyle+O(|zw|)+\varepsilon O((|z|^{2}|+|w|^{2}|)d_{+}^{-1/2})

\displaystyle+O(|\xi|^{3}_{*}d_{+}^{-1/2})+O(|z|^{4})+\varepsilon^{3}O_{2}(d_{+}^{-3}).

This implies that

\displaystyle\dot{\eta}

\displaystyle=(B(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon})+O(\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2}))\eta

\displaystyle+O((\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2})(\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3})+\varepsilon O((\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2})^{2})\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2})

\displaystyle+O((\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2})^{2})(\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3})d_{+}^{-1/2})+O((\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2})^{4})+\varepsilon^{3}O_{2}(\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}).

This in turn implies that

\displaystyle\dot{\eta}=(B(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon})+O(\varepsilon^{1/3}))\eta+\alpha_{d},\ |\alpha_{d}|<c_{b,6}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3}.

(15.15)

Each point in the domain
S(T)\cap D_{q,r,d}
can be reached from the axis
\operatorname{Re}\tau=\operatorname{Re}\tau_{c}
by motion along a curve
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
. Part of this curve is inside the domain
S(T)\cap D_{q,r,d}
. For change in
\eta
along this part, we can use equation
(
15.15
). It could be that a part of this curve is outside
S(T)\cap D_{q,r,d}
. At this part, according to the equation (
11.7
), we have

\displaystyle\dot{\eta}=B(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon})\eta+\varepsilon^{3}\tilde{O}_{2}(d_{+}^{-3}),\ |\tilde{O}_{2}(d_{+}^{-3})|<2c_{r,2}d_{+}^{-3}.

(15.16)

Thus, in both cases

\displaystyle\dot{\eta}=(B(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon})+O(\varepsilon^{1/3}))\eta+\tilde{\alpha}_{d},\ |\tilde{\alpha}_{d}|<c_{b,7}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3}.

(15.17)

Lemma 15.1

In the part of
S(T)
covered by the curves
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
passing through
D_{q,r,d}
, we have

|\eta(t)|<c_{r,7}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3}.

(15.18)

We omit the proof of this lemma because it is analogous to the proof of Lemma
13.5
below.

The obtained estimate for
\eta(t)
allows for an improvement of the pervious estimates related to
z(t)
.
We now obtain in (
15.12
), (
15.13
)

|\alpha_{3}|=O((\varepsilon^{3}d_{+.q}^{-3})^{2})<c_{b,2}\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6},\alpha_{a}=c_{b,4}(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{u}^{-7/2}).

We redefine
\tilde{D}_{q,r,d}
,
\tilde{\Gamma}_{q,r,d}
and
\tilde{\Gamma}_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
using this value of

\alpha_{a}
. On
\tilde{\Gamma}_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
and in
D_{q,r,d}\setminus\tilde{D}_{q,r,d}
, we have

|z(t)|<2\alpha_{a}/\nu<c_{b,5}(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}\hat{d}_{u}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{u}^{-4}).

Now vertical distances between
\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon}
and
\tilde{\Gamma}_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
are

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\varepsilon\nu^{-1}|\ln\left(\frac{\alpha_{a}}{\nu u(s_{0})}\right)|=\varepsilon c_{b,3}^{-1}\hat{d_{u}}^{-1/2}|\ln\left(\frac{c_{b,3}c_{b,4}(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{u}^{-7/2})}{c_{a,3}\hat{d_{u}}^{1/2}\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2}}\right)|

(15.19)

\displaystyle<\varepsilon c_{b,3}^{-1}\hat{d_{u}}^{-1/2}|\ln\left(\frac{c_{b,3}c_{b,4}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{u}^{-7/2}}{c_{a,3}\hat{d_{u}}^{1/2}\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2}}\right)|<c_{b,7}\varepsilon\hat{d_{u}}^{-1/2}|\ln\left(c_{b,6}\varepsilon^{8/3}\hat{d}_{u}^{-4}C_{q}^{5/2}\right)|

\displaystyle=c_{b,9}\varepsilon\hat{d_{u}}^{-1/2}|\ln\left(c_{b,8}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16}\right)|.

Vertical distance between
\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon}
and
\tilde{\Gamma}_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
on the line
\operatorname{Re}\tau=\operatorname{Re}\tau_{c}
is less than

c_{b,10}\varepsilon^{2/3}C_{q}^{-1/2}\ln(C_{q}).

Choose
c_{b,7}
such that
c_{b,10}>c_{e,9,1}
(constant
c_{e,9,1}
is introduced in the statement of Lemma
6.7
).
Denote
c_{e,12,1}=c_{b,8},c_{e,12,2}=c_{b,9}
. Redefine
\tilde{D}_{q,r,d}
,
\tilde{\Gamma}_{q,r,d}
and
\tilde{\Gamma}_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
again using value

c_{e,12,2}\varepsilon\hat{d_{u}}^{1/2}|\ln\left(c_{e,12,1}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16}\right)|
to determine the vertical distances between
\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon}
and
\tilde{\Gamma}_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
.

Now we use this to improve the estimate for
\eta(t)
in
D_{q,r,d}\setminus\tilde{D}_{q,r,d}
. Instead of estimate in (
15.15
), we now have (see Appendix 1)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle|\alpha_{d}|=O(|zw|)+\varepsilon O((|z|^{2}|+|w|^{2}|)d_{+}^{-1/2})+O((|w|^{3}+|w^{2}z|+|wz^{2}|)d_{+}^{-1/2})

(15.20)

\displaystyle+O(|z|^{4})+\varepsilon^{3}O_{2}(d_{+}^{-3})=\varepsilon^{3}O(d_{+}^{-3}).

This estimate allows an improvement of the estimate for
|\eta(t)|
in
D_{q,r,d}\setminus\tilde{D}_{q,r,d}
.

Lemma 15.2

In
S(T)\cap(D_{q,r,d}\setminus\tilde{D}_{q,r,d})
, we have

|\eta(t)|<c_{r,10}\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}.

(15.21)

This is one of estimates stated in Lemma
13.3
.

The obtained estimate for
\eta(t)
allows to improve the estimate for
z(t)
on the curve

\Gamma_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
. For equation (
15.12
) we now have

\displaystyle|\alpha_{3}|=\varepsilon O((\varepsilon^{17/6}d_{+}^{-3})^{2}d_{+}^{-1/2})+O((\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3})(\varepsilon^{17/6}d_{+}^{-3}))+O((\varepsilon^{17/6}d_{+}^{-3})^{3}d_{+}^{-1/2})

\displaystyle+O(\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}(\varepsilon^{17/6}d_{+}^{-3}))=o(\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-7/2}).

Solutions of equation (
15.12
) should now be compared with solutions of equation (
17.1
) in which
\alpha_{a}=c_{b,11}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{u}^{-7/2}
. For this comparison, we take

s_{0}\in\tilde{\Gamma}_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
and initial condition
u(s_{0})=c_{b,12}(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}\hat{d}_{u}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{u}^{-4})
. We have

\frac{\nu u(s_{0})}{\alpha_{a}}<c_{b,3}^{-1}c_{b,11}^{-1}c_{b,12}\left(\frac{(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}\hat{d}_{u}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{u}^{-4})}{\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{u}^{-7/2}}{\hat{d}_{u}^{1/2}}\right)=c_{b,3}^{-1}c_{b,11}^{-1}c_{b,12}\left(\frac{C_{q}^{-6}}{\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-7/2}}+1\right).

If this value is less than 1, then, according to Lemma
17.1
,
u(s)<{\alpha_{a}}/\nu
for any
s\geq s_{0}
. If this value is greater than 1, then, according to Lemma
17.1
,
u(s)<2{\alpha_{a}}/\nu
for

s\geq s_{0}+(1/\nu)|\ln(\alpha_{a}/(\nu u(s_{0}))|
. In this case, we have, assuming that

{C_{q}^{-6}}/{\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-7/2}}>1
,

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\ln\left(\frac{\nu u(s_{0})}{\alpha_{a}}\right)=\ln\left(c_{b,3}^{-1}c_{b,11}^{-1}c_{b,12}\left(\frac{C_{q}^{-6}}{\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-7/2}}+1\right)\right)<c_{b,13}\ln\left(\frac{C_{q}^{-6}}{\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-7/2}}\right)=\frac{7}{3}c_{b,13}\ln\left(\frac{C_{q}^{-6}}{\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-7/2}}\right)^{3/7}

(15.22)

\displaystyle=\frac{7}{3}c_{b,13}\ln\left(\frac{C_{q}^{-18/7}}{\varepsilon^{3/7}\hat{d}_{u}^{-3/2}}\right)<c_{b,14}\ln\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16}}\right).

According to Lemmas
17.1
and
17.2
, in both cases, for

s\geq s_{0}+c_{b,15}\hat{d}_{u}^{-1/2}|\ln(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})|

we have

|z(s)|<2\alpha_{a}/\nu=c_{b,16}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{u}^{-4}.

At this point, we introduce constants
c_{e,11},c_{e,12}
. We take them such that the sum of the vertical distances given by estimates (
15.19
) and (
15.22
) is less than

c_{e,12}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-1/2}|\ln(c_{e,11}^{-1}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})|
. We then redefine the curve
\Gamma_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
so that its vertical distances from the curve
\Gamma_{q,\varepsilon}
are equal to
c_{e,12}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-1/2}|\ln(c_{e,11}^{-1}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})|
. On the curve

\Gamma_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
, we have
|z(t)|<c_{r,9}\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-4}
, as stated
in Lemma
13.3
.

\square

Proof of Lemma
13.4
.

Each point in the domain
S(T)\setminus(D_{q,r,d}\cup\bar{D}_{q,r,d})
can be reached by moving vertically downward from the curve
\Gamma_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
. For such a motion, the equation for
z
in (
11.7
) for
\operatorname{Im}\tau>-c_{l,1}^{-1}
takes the form

\frac{dz}{ds}=-i\Lambda_{1}(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon})z+\alpha,\ |\alpha|<c_{b,1}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-7/2},\ s=-\operatorname{Im}t.

(15.23)

Let
z(s)
denote solution to this equation. For the initial condition on
\Gamma_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
, we have
|z(s_{0})|<c_{r,9}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4}
. Condition 6) in Section
2
implies that
\operatorname{Re}(-i\Lambda_{1}(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t))<-c_{b,2}^{-1}\hat{d}_{+}^{1/2}
.

The solution of equation (
15.23
) can then be compared with the solution of equation

\frac{du}{ds}=-\nu(\varepsilon s)u+\tilde{\alpha}(\varepsilon s),\ \nu=-c_{b,2}^{-1}\hat{d}_{+}^{1/2},\ \tilde{\alpha}=c_{b,1}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-7/2}

(15.24)

with the initial condition
u(s_{0})=c_{r,9}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4}
at
s=s_{0}
.

On some time interval

[s_{0},s_{*})
, we have
|u(s)|<c_{b,3}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4},\ c_{b,3}=\max(c_{r,9},3c_{b,2}c_{b,1})
. Let
s_{*}
be the first value of
s
for which this inequality is not satisfied. At
s=s_{*}
, we have

\frac{du}{ds}=-c_{b,2}^{-1}\hat{d}_{+}^{1/2}c_{b,3}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4}+c_{b,1}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-7/2}<-c_{b,2}^{-1}\hat{d}_{+}^{1/2}2c_{b,2}c_{b,1}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4}+c_{b,1}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-7/2}=-c_{b,1}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-7/2}.

At this value of
s
,

\frac{d}{ds}\left(c_{b,3}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4}\right)<c_{b,4}\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-5}<c_{b,1}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-7/2},

if
c_{e,10}
is sufficiently large. Thus,
u(s)-c_{b,3}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4}
decays at
s=s_{*}
and, therefore, is positive just before
s_{*}
. This contradicts the definition of
s_{*}
. Therefore, the inequality
|u(s)|<c_{b,3}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4}
is satisfied at least while
\operatorname{Im}\tau>-c_{l,1}^{-1}
. Comparing solutions of equations (
15.23
) and (
15.24
), we obtain that the inequality
|z(s)|<c_{b,3}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4}
holds at least while
\operatorname{Im}\tau>-c_{l,1}^{-1}
. In a similar way, we obtain that the inequality

|z(s)|<c_{b,5}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{-}^{-3}
holds for
\operatorname{Im}\tau<c_{l,1}^{-1}
up to reaching the boundary of the domain

\bar{D}_{q,r,d}
.

\square

Proof of Lemma
13.5
.

Each point in the domain
(S(T)\setminus(D_{q,r,d}\cup\bar{D}_{q,r,d})\cap\{\operatorname{Im}\tau\geq-c_{l,1}^{-1}\}
can be reached from the axis
\operatorname{Re}\tau=\operatorname{Re}\tau_{c}
by moving along a curve
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
. Some portions of this curve could pass through the domain
D_{q,r,d}
. We replace these portions by segments of the curve
\Gamma_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
having the same endpoints. By analyticity, there can be only a finite number of such segments. Thus, each point in the domain
(S(T)\setminus(D_{q,r,d}\cup\bar{D}_{q,r,d})\cap\{\operatorname{Im}\tau\geq-c_{l,1}^{-1}\}

can be reached from the axis
\operatorname{Re}\tau=\operatorname{Re}\tau_{c}
by moving along a curve obtained by gluing together pieces of the curves

\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
and
\Gamma_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
.

Consider, in the domain
(S(T)\setminus(D_{q,r,d}\cup\bar{D}_{q,r,d})\cap\{\operatorname{Im}\tau\geq-c_{l,1}^{-1}\}
, a segment of a level curve
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
.
Let the left endpoint of this segment lies on the line
\operatorname{Re}\tau=\operatorname{Re}\tau_{c}
. The right endpoint lies either on
\Gamma_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
, on the line
\operatorname{Im}\tau=-c_{l,1}^{-1}
, or on the line
\operatorname{Re}t=T
.
Introduce the arc-length parameter
\sigma
along this curve
as a new time variable (
\sigma=0
at
\operatorname{Re}\tau=\operatorname{Re}\tau_{c}
). Thus,
t=t(\sigma),\ |dt/d\sigma|=1
. The equation for
\eta
on the curve
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
takes the form

\frac{d\eta}{d\sigma}=\frac{dt}{d\sigma}B(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon})\eta+\alpha,\ \alpha=\varepsilon^{3}\frac{dt}{d\sigma}\tilde{O}_{2}(d_{+}^{-3}),\ |\alpha|<2c_{r,2}\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}.

(15.25)

According to condition 5) in Section
2
, all eigenvalues of the matrix
({dt}/{d\sigma})B(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon})
have negative real parts.

According to
[
4
]
, Sect 13, the homogeneous system with frozen
\tau
has a quadratic Lyapunov function
W(\eta)
whose
\sigma
-derivative for frozen
\tau
is
-(\eta\cdot\bar{\eta})
.
We have
c_{b,1}^{-1}|\eta|^{2}\leq W(\eta)\leq c_{b,1}|\eta|^{2}
. The derivative of
W
in the original system is

\frac{dW}{d\sigma}=-(\eta\cdot\bar{\eta})+O(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2})W_{1}(\eta)+W_{2}(\alpha,\eta)

with a hermitian quadratic form
W_{1}
and a hermitian bilinear form
W_{2}
.

We have
|W_{2}(\alpha,\eta)|\leq c_{b,2}|\alpha||\eta|\leq c_{b,3}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}\sqrt{W}
. This implies that

\frac{dW}{d\sigma}\leq-c_{b,4}^{-1}W+c_{b,5}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}\sqrt{W}.

Denote
v=\sqrt{W}
. Then

\frac{dv}{d\sigma}\leq-c_{b,6}^{-1}v+c_{b,7}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}.

For the starting point on the axis
\operatorname{Re}\tau=\operatorname{Re}\tau_{c}
we have
v<c_{b,8}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}
. Take

c_{r,10}=\max\{c_{b,8},3c_{b,6}c_{b,7}\}
.
Consider the auxiliary equation

\frac{d\tilde{v}}{d\sigma}=-c_{b,6}^{-1}\tilde{v}+c_{b,7}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}

with the initial condition
\tilde{v}=c_{b,8}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}
at
\sigma=0
. Then, on the whole considered interval of
\sigma
, we have
\tilde{v}<c_{r,10}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}
. Indeed, assume that there exists
\sigma_{*}
such that
\tilde{v}=c_{r,10}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}
for the first time. At
\sigma=\sigma_{*}
, we have

\frac{d}{d\sigma}(\tilde{v}-c_{r,10}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3})\leq-c_{b,6}^{-1}2c_{b,6}c_{b,7}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}+c_{b,7}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}+O(\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4})<0,

which contradicts definition of
\sigma_{*}
. We also have
v<\tilde{v}
.

If the right endpoint of the considered segment of the curve
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
lies on
\Gamma_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
, we introduce
\sigma
as the arc length parameter along
\Gamma_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
. We again obtain an equation of the form (
15.25
). If
C_{q}
is sufficiently large, then all eigenvalues of matrix
({dt}/{d\sigma})B
have negative real parts. Indeed, according to Lemma
11.5
, for sufficiently large
C_{q}
,
the tangent directions of the curves
\Gamma_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
and
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
are close to each other at each point of their intersection.
Thus, we can treat a segment of the curve
\Gamma_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
in exactly the same way as a segment of the curve
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
. In this way, we obtain the result of the lemma for
\operatorname{Im}\tau>-c_{l,1}^{-1}
.

The property
\eta(\bar{t})=\bar{\eta}(t)
implies the conclusion of the lemma for
\operatorname{Im}\tau<c_{l,1}^{-1}
.

\square

Proof of Lemma
13.6
.

For
\operatorname{Re}\tau=\operatorname{Re}\tau_{c},\ \operatorname{Im}\tau>\-c_{l,1}^{-1}
, according to Lemma
6.6
, we have

|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|<c_{e,6}\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-9/2}.

According to (
5.7
),

\displaystyle\dot{\kappa}=\varepsilon F(\kappa)+\varepsilon^{2}O(|z|^{2}d_{+}^{-2})+\varepsilon^{2}O(|\xi|_{*}^{2}d_{+}^{-3/2})

\displaystyle+\varepsilon O\left(|z|^{4}+|z|^{5}d_{+}^{-2}+|\xi|_{*}^{3}\right)+\varepsilon^{3}O(|z|d_{+}^{-3}+|\xi|_{*}d_{+}^{-3/2})+\varepsilon^{4}O(d_{+}^{-7/2}|\xi|)

According to the previous lemmas, in the domain covered by curves
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
passing through
D_{q,r,d}
, this reduces to

\displaystyle\dot{\kappa}=\varepsilon F(\kappa)+\varepsilon^{2}O((\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2})^{2}d_{+}^{-2})+\varepsilon^{2}O((\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3})(\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2})d_{+}^{-3/2})

\displaystyle+\varepsilon O\left((\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2})^{4}+(\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2})^{5}d_{+}^{-2}+(\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2})^{2}(\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3})\right)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{3}O((\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2})d_{+}^{-3}+(\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3})d_{+}^{-3/2})+\varepsilon^{4}O((\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2})d_{+}^{-7/2})

\displaystyle=\varepsilon F(\kappa)+O(\varepsilon^{6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5}\hat{d}_{+}^{-2}+\varepsilon^{41/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-11/2}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{9}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-10}+\varepsilon^{11}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-25/2}\hat{d}_{+}^{-2}+\varepsilon^{47/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-8}+\varepsilon^{5}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}+\varepsilon^{35/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2}+\varepsilon^{6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5/2}\hat{d}_{+}^{-7/2}).

(Here we use the fact that we know the estimate for
w
outside
D_{q,r,d}
due to Lemma
13.4
.)
This gives

\displaystyle|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|=O(\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-9/2}+\varepsilon^{5}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-6}+\varepsilon^{35/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-6}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{8}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-10}+\varepsilon^{10}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-27/2}+\varepsilon^{41/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-8}+\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-9/2}+\varepsilon^{29/6}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-7/2}+\varepsilon^{5}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-5})=O(\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+,q}^{-9/2}).

\square

Proof of Lemma
13.7
.

According to Lemmas
13.4
and
13.5
, on the upper boundary of domain
\bar{D}_{q,r,d}\setminus D_{q,r,d}
we have

|z(t)<c_{r,16}\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{3},\ |\eta(t)|<c_{r,16}\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{3},\ |\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|<c_{r,17}\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{-}^{-9/2}

According (
5.8
), in
S(T)\cap\bar{D}_{q,r,d}\setminus D_{q,r,d}
we have

\displaystyle\dot{z}

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{1}(\kappa)z+O(|\eta|(|\eta|+|w|)+\varepsilon O(|z||w|d_{-}^{-3/2})+\varepsilon O(|\xi|^{2}d_{-}^{-1/2})+O(|\xi|_{**}^{3}d_{-}^{-1/2})+O(|w|^{3})

(15.26)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{3}O(d_{-}^{-3}|\xi|)+\varepsilon^{3}O^{*}(d_{-}^{-3}).

Here
|\xi|_{**}^{3}=|\xi|^{3}-|w|^{3}
.
This implies that

\dot{z}=(\Lambda_{1}(\kappa)+\beta)z+\alpha,

where

\displaystyle\beta=O(\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{2}d_{-,q}^{-5/2})d_{-}^{-3/2})+O(\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{2}d_{-,q}^{-5/2})d_{-}^{-1/2})+O((\varepsilon^{2}d_{-,q}^{-5/2})^{2}d_{-}^{-1/2})+O(\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-3}),

(15.27)

\displaystyle\alpha=O((\varepsilon^{2}d_{-,q}^{-5/2})(\varepsilon^{17/6}d_{-,q}^{-3}))+O(\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{2}d_{-,q}^{-5/2})^{2}d_{-}^{-1/2})+O((\varepsilon^{2}d_{-,q}^{-5/2})^{2}(\varepsilon^{17/6}d_{-,q}^{-3})d_{-}^{-1/2})

\displaystyle+O((\varepsilon^{2}d_{-,q}^{-5/2})^{3}+\varepsilon^{3}O(d_{-}^{-3}).

This gives

|\beta|<c_{b,1}\varepsilon^{1/3},\quad|\alpha|<c_{b,2}\varepsilon^{3}d_{-,q}^{-3}.

Lemma
13.6
implies that

\Lambda_{1}(\kappa(t))=\Lambda_{1}(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t))+O(\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{-,q}^{-5})=\Lambda_{1}(\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t))+O(\varepsilon^{2/3}).

in
\bar{D}_{q,r,d}
.

Considering downward motion along the lines
\operatorname{Re}t={\rm const}
, similarly to proof of Lemma

13.4
, we obtain
|z|<c_{r,7}\varepsilon^{3}d_{-,q}^{-3}
in the domain
\bar{D}_{q,r,d}\setminus D_{q,r,d}
.

In the domain
\bar{D}_{q,r,d}\setminus\overline{\tilde{D}}_{q,r,d}
one can obtain better estimate using the bounds

|w(t)|<c_{r,9}(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}\hat{d}_{-}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{-}^{-4}),\ |\eta(t)|<c_{r,10}\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}

valid there. This gives (see Appendix 1)

|\alpha|=O(\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{-}^{-3}),

(15.28)

which implies that

|z(t)|<c_{r,13}\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-3}.

\square

Proof of Lemma
13.8
.

According to (
5.7
), in the domain
D_{q,r,d}
we have

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\dot{\kappa}=\varepsilon F(\kappa)+\varepsilon^{2}O(|z|^{2}d_{+}^{-2})+\varepsilon^{2}O(|\xi|_{*}^{2}d_{+}^{-3/2})

(15.29)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon O\left(|z|^{4}+|z|^{5}d_{+}^{-2}+|\xi|_{*}^{3}\right)+\varepsilon^{3}O(|z|d_{+}^{-3}+|\xi|_{*}d_{+}^{-3/2})+\varepsilon^{4}O(d_{+}^{-7/2}|\xi|).

On
\Gamma_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
, we have

|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|<c_{r,17}\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-9/2}.

(15.30)

We estimate
|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|
in
D_{q,r,d}^{\prime}\setminus\tilde{D}_{q,r,d}
by moving vertically upward from
\Gamma_{q,r,d}^{\prime}
.
In
D_{q,r,d}^{\prime}\setminus\tilde{D}_{q,r,d}
, we have the estimates

|z(t)|<c_{r,9}(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4}),\ |\eta(t)|<\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3},\ |w(t)|<\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}.

The vertical width of the domain
D_{q,r,d}^{\prime}\setminus\tilde{D}_{q,r,d}
is less than
c_{e,12}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-1/2}|\ln(c_{e,11}^{-1}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{u}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})|
.
We know that

\partial F(\kappa)/\partial\kappa=O(\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2})
.
According to Lemmas
11.3
,
11.4
, on the vertical line,
d_{u}
can be replaced with
\hat{d}_{+}
in
O(\cdot)
-estimates.
Then (
15.29
), (
15.30
) for
\tau\in D^{\prime}_{q,r,d}\setminus\tilde{D}_{q,r,d}
imply

\displaystyle|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|=O(\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-9/2})+\left(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}|\ln(c_{e,11}^{-1}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})|\right)

\displaystyle\cdot O\left(\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4})^{2}\hat{d}_{+}^{-2}+\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4})(\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3})\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2}\right.

\displaystyle+(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4})^{4}+(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4})^{5}\ \hat{d}_{+}^{-2}+(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4})^{2}(\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3})

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4})\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}+\varepsilon^{2}(\varepsilon^{17/6}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3})\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2}+\left.\varepsilon^{3}(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+})\hat{d}_{+}^{-7/2}\right).

The largest terms in this expression arise from the terms
\varepsilon^{2}O(|z|^{2}d_{+}^{-2})
and
\varepsilon^{3}O(|z|d_{+}^{-3}
in (
15.29
).
We estimate the ratios of the contributions of these terms to
|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|
relative to
\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-9/2}
:

\displaystyle\frac{\left(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}|\ln(c_{e,11}^{-1}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})|\right)\varepsilon\left(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4}\right)^{2}\hat{d}_{+}^{-2}}{\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-9/2}}

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{\left(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}|\ln(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})|\right)\varepsilon\left(\varepsilon^{4}C_{q}^{-12}\hat{d}_{+}^{-1}+\varepsilon^{6}\hat{d}_{+}^{-8}\right)\hat{d}_{+}^{-2}}{\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-9/2}}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{\left(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}|\ln(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})|\right)\varepsilon\left(\varepsilon^{6}\hat{d}_{+}^{-8}\right)\hat{d}_{+}^{-2}}{\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-9/2}}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-6}|\ln(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})|\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-6}|\ln(\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-6}C_{q}^{15/4})|\right)=O(C_{q}^{-6}|\ln(C_{q})|)=O(1).

\displaystyle\frac{\left(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}|\ln(c_{e,11}^{-1}\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})|\right)\varepsilon^{2}\left(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4}\right)\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}}{\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-9/2}}

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{\left(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-1/2}|\ln(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})|\right)\varepsilon^{2}\left(\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4}\right)\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}}{\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-9/2}}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}|\ln(\varepsilon\hat{d}_{+}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{15/16})|\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}|\ln(\varepsilon^{2}\hat{d}_{+}^{-3}C_{q}^{15/8})|\right)=O(C_{q}^{-3}|\ln(C_{q})|)=O(1).

Contributions of the remaining terms are smaller; their ratios to
\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-9/2}
tends to 0 as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Thus,

|\kappa(t)-\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon t)|<c_{r,11}\varepsilon^{4}\hat{d}_{+}^{-9/2}.

\square

16
Proofs for Lemmas from Section 15

Proof of Lemma
15.2
.

Each point in the domain
S(T)\cap(D_{q,r,d}\setminus\tilde{D}_{q,r,d})
can be reached from the axis
\operatorname{Re}\tau=\operatorname{Re}\tau_{c}
by moving along a curve glued from pieces of the curves
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
,
\tilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}=\tilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{q,r,d}
, and
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma^{\prime}_{q,r,d}
. According to condition 6) in Section
2
, the linearised near the equilibrium fast system, considered along any curve
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
, has
n-2
eigenvalues with negative real parts, corresponding to variables
\eta
. The same is valid for this system considered along the curves
\tilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}
, if
C_{q}
is sufficiently large.
This follows because, according to Lemma
11.5
, by choosing
C_{q}

sufficiently large, the tangent directions of the curve
\tilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}
(respectively,
\Gamma^{\prime}
) and of the curve
\operatorname{Re}\Psi_{\varepsilon}={\rm const}
passing through the same point can be made arbitrarily close. On this basis, the result of Lemma
15.2
can be obtained using the Lyapunov function, in the same way as in the proof of Lemma
13.5
. We omit the details.

\square
