The work was supported by the EPSRC, Grant No. UKRI1724. We are grateful to A. P. Veselov for
useful discussion.

17
Appendix 1. Auxiliary estimates.

Lemma 17.1

Consider the linear ODE with constant coefficients for a real variable
u
with real independent variable
s
:

\frac{du}{ds}=-\nu u+\alpha_{a},\ \nu>0,\ \alpha_{a}>0.

(17.1)

Consider the solution
u(s)
of this equation with the initial condition
u(s_{0})>0
. Then the following estimates hold.

(a) If
u(s_{0})<\alpha_{a}/\nu
, then
u(s)<\alpha_{a}/\nu
for
s\geq s_{0}
.

(b) If
u(s_{0})\geq\alpha_{a}/\nu
, then
u(s)\leq u(s_{0})
for
s\geq s_{0}
.
For
s\geq s_{0}+(1/\nu)|\ln(\alpha_{a}/(\nu u(s_{0}))|
, we have
u(s)<2\alpha_{a}/\nu
.

Lemma 17.2

Consider the following linear ODEs for a complex variable
z
and a real variable
u
, with real independent variable
s\in[s_{0},s_{1}]
:

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\frac{dz}{ds}=-\nu_{1}z+\alpha_{a,1},

(17.2)

\displaystyle\frac{du}{ds}=-\nu u+\alpha_{a},\ \nu>0,\alpha_{a}>0.

Here
\nu_{1}
and
\alpha_{a,1}
are complex-valued functions, while
\nu
and
\alpha_{a}
are real-valued functions such that
\operatorname{Re}\nu_{1}>\nu,\,|\alpha_{a,1}|<\alpha_{a}
. Let
z(s),u(s)
be solutions of these equations such that
|z(s_{0})|\leq u(s_{0})
. Then
|z(s)|<u(s)
for
s\in(s_{0},s_{1}]
.

Proof of Lemma
17.1
.

The solution to the equation (
17.1
) is

u(s)=\frac{\alpha_{a}}{\nu}+e^{-\nu(s-s_{0})}\left(u(s_{0})-\frac{\alpha_{a}}{\nu}\right).

Thus,

u(s)-u(s_{0})=\left(\frac{\alpha_{a}}{\nu}-u(s_{0})\right)\left(1-e^{-\nu(s-s_{0})}\right).

(a) For
u(s_{0})<{\alpha_{a}}/{\nu}
, this implies that
u(s)<{\alpha_{a}}/{\nu}
.

(b) For
u(s_{0})\geq{\alpha_{a}}/{\nu}
, this implies that
u(s)\leq u(s_{0})
.
Additionally, for
s\geq s_{0}+(1/\nu)|\ln(\alpha_{a}/(\nu u(s_{0}))|
, we have

u(s)<\frac{\alpha_{a}}{\nu}+\frac{\alpha_{a}}{\nu u(s_{0})}u(s_{0})=2\frac{\alpha_{a}}{\nu}.

\square

Proof of Lemma
17.2
.

For
|z|\neq 0
, we have

\frac{d|z|}{ds}=-\operatorname{Re}(\nu_{1})|z|+\frac{1}{2|z|}(\alpha_{a,1}\bar{z}+\bar{\alpha}_{a,1}z)\leq-\operatorname{Re}(\nu_{1})|z|+|\alpha_{a,1}|.

The proof then follows the standard argument for the comparison lemma for solutions of one-dimensional real ODEs. For
s>s_{0}
sufficiently close to
s_{0}
, we have
|z(s)|<u(s)
. Let
s_{*}
be the first time such that
|z(s)|=u(s)
. At this time we must have
d|z|/ds\geq du/ds
. However, this can’t occur, because
|z(s_{*})|=u(s_{*})
implies
d|z|/ds<du/ds
.

\square

Proof of estimate for
\alpha_{d}
in (
15.20
).

We have

\displaystyle|\alpha_{d}|=O(|zw|)+\varepsilon O((|z|^{2}|+|w|^{2}|)d_{+}^{-1/2})+O((|w|^{3}+|w^{2}z|+|wz^{2}|)d_{+}^{-1/2})

\displaystyle+O(|z|^{4})+\varepsilon^{3}O_{2}(d_{+}^{-3}),

and, according to (
15.14
) and (
11.25
),

|z(t)|<c_{b,5}(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}{d}_{+}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{+}^{-4}),\ |w|<\varepsilon^{17/6}{d}_{+}^{-3}.

We should prove that
|\alpha_{d}|=\varepsilon^{3}O(d_{+}^{-3})
.

The estimates can be carried out separately for
|z|\sim|z_{a}|=O(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}{d}_{+}^{-1/2})
and

|z|\sim|z_{b}|=O(\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-4})
.

For
|z|\sim|z_{a}|=O(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}{d}_{+}^{-1/2})
.

\displaystyle\frac{|z_{a}w|}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}}=O\left(\frac{(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}{d}_{+}^{-1/2})(\varepsilon^{17/6}{d}_{+}^{-3})}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{11/6}{d}_{+}^{-1/2}C_{q}^{-6}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{3/2}C_{q}^{-13/2}\right)\ll 1,

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon|z_{a}^{2}|d_{+}^{-1/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}}=O\left(\frac{\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}{d}_{+}^{-1/2})^{2}{d}_{+}^{-1/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{2}d_{+}^{3/2}C_{q}^{-12}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-12}\right)\ll 1,

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon|w^{2}|d_{+}^{-1/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}}=O\left(\frac{\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{17/6}{d}_{+}^{-3})^{2}{d}_{+}^{-1/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{11/3}d_{+}^{-7/2}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{4/3}C_{q}^{-7/2}\right)\ll 1,

\displaystyle\frac{|w^{3}|d_{+}^{-1/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}}=O\left(\frac{(\varepsilon^{17/6}{d}_{+}^{-3})^{3}{d}_{+}^{-1/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{11/2}{d}_{+}^{-13/2}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{7/6}C_{q}^{-13/2}\right)\ll 1,

\displaystyle\frac{|w^{2}z_{a}|d_{+}^{-1/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}}=O\left(\frac{(\varepsilon^{17/6}{d}_{+}^{-3})^{2}(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}{d}_{+}^{-1/2}){d}_{+}^{-1/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{14/3}{d}_{+}^{-4}C_{q}^{-6}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-10}\right)\ll 1,

\displaystyle\frac{|wz_{a}^{2}|d_{+}^{-1/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}}=O\left(\frac{(\varepsilon^{17/6}{d}_{+}^{-3})(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}{d}_{+}^{-1/2})^{2}{d}_{+}^{-1/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{23/6}{d}_{+}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{-12}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{17/6}C_{q}^{-27}\right)\ll 1,

\displaystyle\frac{|z_{a}^{4}|}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}}=O\left(\frac{(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}{d}_{+}^{-1/2})^{4}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{5}{d}_{+}C_{q}^{-24}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{5}C_{q}^{-24}\right)\ll 1.

For
|z|\sim|z_{b}|=O(\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-4})
.

\displaystyle\frac{|z_{b}w|}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}}=O\left(\frac{(\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-4})(\varepsilon^{17/6}{d}_{+}^{-3})}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{17/6}{d}_{+}^{-4}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{1/6}C_{q}^{-4}\right)\ll 1,

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon|z_{b}^{2}|d_{+}^{-1/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}}=O\left(\frac{\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-4})^{2}{d}_{+}^{-1/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{4}{d}_{+}^{-11/2}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{1/3}C_{q}^{-11/2}\right)\ll 1,

\displaystyle\frac{|w^{2}z_{b}|d_{+}^{-1/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}}=O\left(\frac{(\varepsilon^{17/6}{d}_{+}^{-3})^{2}(\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-4}){d}_{+}^{-1/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{17/3}{d}_{+}^{-15/2}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{2/3}C_{q}^{-15/2}\right)\ll 1,

\displaystyle\frac{|wz_{b}^{2}|d_{+}^{-1/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}}=O\left(\frac{(\varepsilon^{17/6}{d}_{+}^{-3})(\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-4})^{2}{d}_{+}^{-1/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{35/6}{d}_{+}^{-17/2}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{1/3}C_{q}^{-17/2}\right)\ll 1,

\displaystyle\frac{|z_{b}^{4}|}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}}=O\left(\frac{(\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{+}^{-4})^{4}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{+}^{-3}}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{9}{d}_{+}^{-13}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{1/3}C_{q}^{-13}\right)\ll 1.

Thus,
|\alpha_{d}|=\varepsilon^{3}O(d_{+}^{-3})
.

Proof of estimate for
\alpha
in (
15.28
).

We have

\displaystyle|\alpha|=O(|\eta|(|\eta|+|w|))+\varepsilon O(|w|^{2}d_{-}^{-1/2})+\varepsilon O(|w\eta|d_{-}^{-1/2})+O(|\eta||w|^{2}d_{-}^{-1/2})

\displaystyle+O(|\eta|^{2}|w|d_{-}^{-1/2})+O(|\eta|^{3})O(|w|^{3})+\varepsilon^{3}O(d_{-}^{-3}|w|)+\varepsilon^{3}O^{*}(d_{-}^{-3}).

and, according to Lemma
13.3
,

|w(t)|<c_{r,9}(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}{d}_{-}^{-1/2}+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{d}_{-}^{-4}),\ |\eta(t)|<c_{r,10}\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{-}^{-3}.

We should prove that
|\alpha|=\varepsilon^{3}O(d_{-}^{-3})
.

The estimates can be carried out separately for
|w|\sim|w_{a}|=O(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}{d}_{-}^{-1/2})
and

|w|\sim|w_{b}|=O(\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{-}^{-4})
.

For
|w|\sim|w_{a}|=O(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}{d}_{-}^{-1/2})
.

\displaystyle\frac{|\eta w_{a}|}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-3}}=O\left(\frac{(\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-3})(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}{d}_{-}^{-1/2})}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-3}}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}{d}_{-}^{-1/2}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{5/3}C_{q}^{-13/2}\right)\ll 1,

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon|w_{a}|^{2}d_{-}^{-1/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-3}}=O\left(\frac{\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}{d}_{-}^{-1/2})^{2}d_{-}^{-1/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-3}}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{2}d_{-}^{3/2}C_{q}^{-12}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-12}\right)\ll 1,

\displaystyle\frac{\eta|w_{a}|^{2}d_{-}^{-1/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-3}}=O\left(\frac{(\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-3})(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}{d}_{-}^{-1/2})^{2}d_{-}^{-1/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-3}}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{4}d_{-}^{-3/2}C_{q}^{-12}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{3}C_{q}^{-29/2}\right)\ll 1,

\displaystyle\frac{|w_{a}|^{3}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-3}}=O\left(\frac{(\varepsilon^{2}C_{q}^{-6}{d}_{-}^{-1/2})^{3}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-3}}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{3/2}C_{q}^{-18}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{3}C_{q}^{-18}\right)\ll 1.

For
|w|\sim|w_{b}|=O(\varepsilon^{3}{d}_{-}^{-4})
.

\displaystyle\frac{|\eta w_{b}|}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-3}}=O\left(\frac{(\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-3})(\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-4})}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-3}}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-4}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{1/3}C_{q}^{-4}\right)\ll 1,

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon|w_{b}|^{2}d_{-}^{-1/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-3}}=O\left(\frac{\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-4})^{2}d_{-}^{-1/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-3}}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{4}d_{-}^{-11/2}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{1/3}C_{q}^{-11/2}\right)\ll 1,

\displaystyle\frac{\eta|w_{b}|^{2}d_{-}^{-1/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-3}}=O\left(\frac{(\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-3})(\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-4})d_{-}^{-1/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-3}}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-9/2}\right)=O\left(C_{q}^{-9/2}\right)\ll 1,

\displaystyle\frac{|w_{b}|^{3}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-3}}=O\left(\frac{(\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-4})^{3}}{\varepsilon^{3}d_{-}^{-3}}\right)=O\left(\varepsilon^{6}d_{-}^{-9}\right)=O\left(C_{q}^{-9}\right)\ll 1.

Thus,
|\alpha|=\varepsilon^{3}O(d_{-}^{-3})
.
